SR-006Gate/tier harness enforces required stepsre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-006current content
SR-ID
SR-006
Title
Gate/tier harness enforces required steps
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-008
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall run the required steps of the gate that must next be passed (the strictness selector cached in docs/gate) and fail that gate when a required tool is missing, reporting SKIP(missing) rather than silently passing — and, on a claimed work branch (one whose history holds a docs/work/active/<branch>/ claim), SKIP its generated-artifact freshness steps with a stated trunk-lane reason, generated artifacts being trunk-only, while every non-freshness step still runs.
Rationale
Realizes SN-004 (gates enforce their bar), SN-008 (no false green) and the dissolved edge expectation that a missing tool fails, never skips. ABSORBS SR-133, 2026-08-15: that row's own rationale read "Decomposed from SR-006", which is the census's demotion criterion stated by the row about itself — a sanctioned SKIP class of this harness's own step plan is not a second crossing observable, it is the same one qualified. The skip is not a weakening: a work branch cannot commit generated artifacts at all (concurrency-restructure §5.2), so demanding their freshness there would force the very conflict the rule deletes; the skip is reported, never silent, and any signal ambiguity resolves toward the full trunk bar. SR-133 was `Verified`; the demotion overrides that attestation deliberately, under the owner's 2026-08-15 ruling, and is named in the log rather than ridden in quietly.
AcceptanceCriteria
check.py --gate <bar> runs that bar's steps; a required-but-absent tool fails the gate with guidance (never a silent pass), and --lenient degrades it to SKIP; --tier selects the declared step subset; on a claimed work branch the declared freshness steps SKIP with a §5.2 notice and exit code untouched while skills-sync, trajectory and registry-integrity still run; the closing commit and its successors keep the skip (the signal derives from branch history, not the working tree); the trunk lane never skips them. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: check.py.

Title
Per-module coverage-floor comparator
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_coverage.py
CodeSymbol
load_floors/load_report/evaluate
Detail
Reads the docs/coverage-floors census (module + min percent; blank and # lines ignored) and coverage.py's JSON report (per-file percent_covered, path-normalized), fails on any module below its floor or absent from the report, SKIPs when the report is absent, is a no-op with an empty census, and fails loudly on a malformed floor line. A failure names the offending module, and all floors holding exits 0 — the per-module floor is what keeps the global aggregate from hiding a thin high-risk module.

SR-Refs
SR-006;SR-170
Title
Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
CodeSymbol
_claimed_work_branch/steps
Detail
On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
Rationale
A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.

SR-007Declared stack profile, refused when it is brokenre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-007current content
SR-ID
SR-007
Title
Declared stack profile, refused when it is broken
SN-Refs
SN-003
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall read the toolchain (format/lint/test commands, tiers, coverage, arch-map mode) from the declared stack profile so a stack swap edits only that declaration, failing loudly on a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold rather than proceeding on a bad one.
Rationale
Realizes SN-003 — the kit is stack-agnostic; the harness is re-pointed by declaration, not by editing a kit script. ABSORBS SR-008, 2026-08-15: reading a declaration and REFUSING a broken one are one obligation, not two — a reader that proceeds on a malformed profile has not read it. SR-008 named a script and a mechanism, which is the census's demotion criterion, and `trace.py` had already been warning that its only child (LLR-008) "mostly re-words" it, the shape a row takes when it is a design detail wearing an SR id. Its third clause, a MISSING DECLARED BINARY, is not folded here: SR-006 already states it verbatim as its own ("fail that gate when a required tool is missing"), which is why LLR-008 now decomposes from both rows rather than one. SR-008 was `Verified`; the demotion overrides that attestation deliberately, under the owner's 2026-08-15 ruling, and is named in the log rather than ridden in quietly.
AcceptanceCriteria
check.py resolves each step's command from stack.ini; changing the profile changes the commands run, with no script edit; a malformed profile or a non-integer coverage threshold produces a clear nonzero failure at the relevant step rather than a run on a bad profile (a missing declared binary fails under SR-006, which states that clause). Read off the current carrier, as the current set: check.py, docs/stack.ini.

SR SR-011current content
SR-ID
SR-011
Title
Idempotent re-runnable scaffold
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-007
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered scaffold generator shall skip an existing file on a re-run unless an explicit overwrite is requested.
Rationale
Realizes SN-001 (safe drop-in onto an existing repo) and SN-007 — an adopter picking up kit updates must not lose their own edits, and a scaffolder that overwrites cannot be run twice, so it stops being run at all. Overwrite-always with a backup copy was rejected: it leaves the repo carrying a second copy of every file it touched. The deliberate kit-vs-project merge is a separate, operator-driven process (SR-036).
AcceptanceCriteria
A re-run leaves every existing file (kit- or project-owned) byte-unchanged without --force; --force overwrites. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: bootstrap.py --force.

SR-017Always-on secrets floorre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-017current content
SR-ID
SR-017
Title
Always-on secrets floor
SN-Refs
SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
When a session attempts to commit or push content matching a secret pattern, the system shall refuse at the hook floor — scanning every commit's staged diff, message and outgoing range regardless of the privacy toggle, and naming the finding — unless the declared secrets_scan dial is explicitly false.
Rationale
Realizes SN-009 — a credential that reaches a shared branch must be treated as disclosed and rotated, and expunging it from history is a rewrite every clone has to follow, so the cheap moment to catch it is before the commit and the expensive one is any moment after. Folding the scan into the privacy toggle was rejected: that makes the highest-severity check opt-in alongside a preference. The one opt-out is an explicit declaration, so turning it off is a visible act.
AcceptanceCriteria
A staged PEM header, token, or key pattern is flagged and blocks; a clean diff passes; the floor runs with privacy-check off, and is disabled only by an explicit secrets_scan = false.
Priority
M
Verification

SR-018Privacy gate two-axisre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-018current content
SR-ID
SR-018
Title
Privacy gate two-axis
SN-Refs
SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
When the declared privacy_check dial is on, the system shall additionally refuse, at the same hook floor, a commit or push carrying a PII/identity class — exempting configured author identities.
Rationale
Realizes SN-009 — identity and PII classes are a policy choice a repo makes about its own contributors, unlike a leaked credential, which is a defect in every repo. Making them always-on was rejected because the exemption list they need is project-specific: a repo that has not declared its author identities would have the layer firing on its own commit metadata, and the fix would be to silence the scanner rather than to configure it.
AcceptanceCriteria
With privacy-check on, an identity/PII class is flagged unless the author is in EXEMPT_EMAILS; with it off, only the secrets floor runs.

SR-019Pre-commit hook floorre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-019current content
SR-ID
SR-019
Title
Pre-commit hook floor
SN-Refs
SN-005;SN-009
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
The pre-commit hook shall run the integrity + secrets floor on every commit, agent-neutrally — a LOCAL floor, bypassable by the session (git commit --no-verify), so it discharges "no unchecked write enters governed state" only as a PAIR with the hosted re-run of the same bar (SR-151/SR-152), never alone.
Rationale
Realizes SN-005 and SN-009 — a floor only CI enforces is one the local tree crosses freely, and a secret caught after the commit is already in history. Trusting the agent to run the checks was rejected: a floor exists for the times the agent forgets, so it is git-enforced and agent-neutral. Parents the interpreter probe from 2026-08-15 (retired SR-021): a hook that crashes cryptically because no python3 resolves, or because a Windows Store alias exits nonzero, has not run the floor — so probing by RUNNING a candidate and reporting clearly is machinery serving THIS obligation, and it decomposes to LLR-021 rather than standing as a requirement of its own.
AcceptanceCriteria
The hook runs trace --strict-integrity and the secrets scan on staged content and blocks a commit that fails either; a --no-verify commit bypasses it and is caught instead by the hosted re-run, which is what makes the pair — not this row — the honest unit of the claim.
Priority
M
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
1
LLR LLR-019current content
LLR-ID
LLR-019
SR-Refs
SR-019
Title
Pre-commit floor
Module

SR-026Coordinator resumes headlessre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-026current content
SR-ID
SR-026
Title
Coordinator resumes headless
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall resume headless with stdin closed, never blocking on a prompt: a worker resumes from its explicit claimed assignment plus the committed trailer evidence on its branch, and the integrator derives claim and queue state from trunk history alone — the generated status surface never a session input.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run. ACCEPTANCE NARROWED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied): the acceptance carried two obligations the shall never stated - transient-limit backoff and stall abort - and an acceptance cell cannot mint a requirement. Both were MINTED rather than deleted, because each describes live behaviour worth keeping: SR-171 and SR-172. An unattended run blocked on a prompt is indistinguishable from a hung one and holds its lane until a human notices. Keeping a separate serial resume path beside the dispatcher was rejected: two authorities over one decision drift, and the divergence surfaces as a run that resumes differently depending on how it was started.
AcceptanceCriteria
A worker reconciles from its assignment + committed evidence and the integrator from trunk history; every mode runs with stdin closed and a prompt cannot wedge it. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: agent_loop.py.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
1
LLR LLR-026current content
LLR-ID
LLR-026
SR-Refs
SR-026
Title
Headless session launch
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
CodeSymbol
build_argv/run_session/_codex_lastmsg_setup
Detail
Runs the agent CLI headless: build_argv substitutes {model} into argv and, when a CmdTemplate carries no {prompt} placeholder (the default for both provider CLIs), routes the prompt to the child's STDIN (run_session writes it from a daemon thread then closes), while the no-wedge invariant holds — a fixed prompt plus EOF, never an interactive read. A codex session additionally captures that CLI's own --output-last-message file as the result (_codex_lastmsg_setup/_codex_lastmsg_read). stdin is otherwise closed so no prompt wedges the loop; the scope is the dispatcher and worker sessions, and the former status.md-resume read is retired rather than coexisting with them.
Rationale
A large prompt passed as argv dies at the OS command-line cap (8191 characters through a Windows .CMD shim, 32767 via CreateProcess) and a round's briefs run to roughly 70k, so stdin is the only delivery that survives — both limits verified live rather than assumed. The last-message capture exists because that CLI echoes its banner and the whole prompt into stdout, so stdout is not the result.

(see TC-026)

Approved

Title
Explicit WI/train/worktree worker assignment
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
build_worker_assignment/worker_prompt
Detail
Replaces internal --track assumptions with explicit --wi/--train/worktree assignment; assembles the worker prompt from AGENTS.md + WI row + SpecRef + predecessor context + train diff + rework finding (never the generated status surface); collision-safe per-assignment logs/review evidence naming the exact reviewed commit; the --track lane machinery is retired outright. The assignment is the branch integrate.py's claim cut, and the session tag defaults to the branch name; the prompt never includes docs/status.md; a worker branch never edits root status, another branch's claims, the root log or generated artifacts; the result channel is committed trailer evidence (WI: / Blocked-WI: + BlockRef:).

LLR LLR-143current content
LLR-ID
LLR-143
SR-Refs
SR-026
Title
Plain-launch drive loop (claim->build->integrate)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
CodeSymbol
run/_resume_or_claim/_stranded_claims/_default_worker
Detail
The serial scheduling front end a plain agent_loop.py launch runs: each cycle re-derives the ready frontier (schedule.frontier), refuses on the tracked pause and on a dirty trunk at the cycle top, resumes a parked claimed branch else claims the next queued WI in build order (integrate.claim, branch name check-ref-format-validated), launches one worker session on the claimed branch's worktree (agent_loop.py --worktree --wi with the session dials forwarded), drains the serial merge queue (integrate.integrate) counting merges from finished_branches, and stops loudly on any composed refusal - stranded claims (active specs with no branch ref) refuse by name, NEEDS-HUMAN propagates as exit 7, a stall aborts, an empty frontier drains to exit 0. The session-config preflight applies only when work needs a worker. Never pushes.
Rationale
Ordering only, no new authority: every refusal stays where it already lives, and the driver is ~400 lines of composition against the 4,042-line dispatcher it replaces.

SR-027Coordinator preflightre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-027current content
SR-ID
SR-027
Title
Coordinator preflight
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall refuse a broken footing in preflight — not a git repo, no agent CLI, a private author under privacy-check, or a second coordinator in the same checkout while another holds its lock — exiting a typed nonzero code rather than hanging.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that a preflight on a non-git working directory reports and exits nonzero rather than hanging — a run that starts on a broken footing fails later and further from the cause, and an unattended loop cannot be asked what went wrong. Continuing with a warning was rejected: each of these conditions makes every subsequent step meaningless, so a typed nonzero exit is what lets the caller act on it. ABSORBS SR-030, 2026-08-15: a second coordinator in the same checkout is a FOURTH broken footing, not a separate obligation — it fails in the same direction, at the same moment, through the same refusal, and SR-030 named a script and a lock mechanism, which is the census's demotion criterion. Two coordinators in one checkout write the same working tree and the loser's commits interleave into the winner's diff where no review will separate them; coordinating by convention was rejected there for the reason that keeps it here — the failure is silent and shows up only as a garbled history. The lock machinery decomposes to LLR-029/LLR-030.
AcceptanceCriteria
Outside a git repo, with no agent CLI, or with a private author under privacy-check, preflight exits a typed nonzero code; a second coordinator on a held lock is refused so only one writer runs per checkout, preventing a two-writer race. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: agent_loop.py.

SR-028Coordinator typed outcomesre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-028current content
SR-ID
SR-028
Title
Coordinator typed outcomes
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall end each session in a typed outcome code, guard a zero-commit HEAD, and report an all-ERROR stall as an unavailable agent rather than a work stall.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectations that a repository with no commits yet (HEAD absent) does not crash the loop and that an all-ERROR run is an unavailable agent rather than a work stall — a coordinator ending in an untyped code forces its caller to parse prose; a session that produced no commit still has a HEAD to report, so without a guard an empty run is indistinguishable from a productive one in the record it leaves; and an all-ERROR run reported as a work stall sends a human looking for a blocked work item that does not exist. Treating an agent error as a stall was rejected for that reason: an unreachable agent and a blocked item need different responses, so they need different outcomes.
AcceptanceCriteria
Every declared end state returns its assigned outcome code, and an unrecognized end state fails rather than returning a success code; a repo with no commits does not crash the loop; an agent-CLI error is logged ERROR; an all-ERROR region is reported as an unavailable agent. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: agent_loop.py.

SR-040Per-phase routing and review dialre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-040current content
SR-ID
SR-040
Title
Per-phase routing and review dial
SN-Refs
SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered coordinator shall route each in-process session phase (PLAN/BUILD/REVIEW-A/REVIEW-B/DESIGN-CHECK/CRITIQUE) through its declared per-phase command template, falling back to the single declared command — surfacing the declared reviewer dial at run start without enforcing it, and warning (never blocking) when a lane resume surface exceeds the declared size threshold.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 and the dissolved edge expectation that an unattended run never blocks on a prompt, at launch or mid-run — two samples of one model share blind spots, so a review by the family that built the change agrees with it for the wrong reasons; cross-family routing is what makes the second opinion independent, and one model for every phase was rejected on that basis. Dispatch stays keyed off the in-process session phase because the alternative, a per-phase code branch, makes adding a phase a code change and so discourages adding one. The resume surface's growth tripwire warns rather than fails because a growing resume surface is a smell and not a defect, and failing on a smell trains an operator to bypass the check. Hat-derived (hat.UX-ENGINEER + hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): only the resume-surface tripwire clause is orphaned, and two lenses reach it independently — a view must stay usable as its content volume grows (C-UXE-2), and at 3am the reader of a resume surface is another automated step, which needs the growth legible from the artifact rather than from a human noticing (C-UNA-8). Both also explain the warn-not-fail severity: a smell is reported, not gated. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A mapped session phase invokes its own command template and the default template does not fire; a broken map entry fails preflight before iteration 1; the banner surfaces the reviewer dial from docs/process.toml [policies] review_rounds; an oversized status.md warns on stderr while the run proceeds; AGENT_STATUS_WARN_BYTES=0 silences.

SR-043Subagent spawn gatere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-043current content
SR-ID
SR-043
Title
Subagent spawn gate
SN-Refs
SN-006;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-04
Requirement
A PreToolUse subagent-spawn gate shall, during an unattended run, refuse or defer subagent spawns per the declared subagent-gate dial in the single policy declaration home (off|ask|deny; absent=off; the legacy one-word file is still read through the migration window), honor a launcher-set SUBAGENT_GATE=allow override the model cannot set, and fail open on any error so a broken gate never wedges the tools.
Rationale
Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run stays safe — bounded, supervised fan-out with the override held by the human, not the model) and SN-012 (opt-in: absent/off is a vacuous allow, so a non-adopter pays nothing). Supervision, not security — a model that can edit files can remove the hook (adapted from stop-subagent-fanout, MIT; spec docs/archive/INTEGRATION_PLAN.md Phase 4). Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-2 asks that the irreversible actions an unattended run can take be enumerated, each naming the dial or human authority that permits it — which is this row exactly, for the one action that starts further actors. A direct hit, and the reason this row is not accretion: its parentage was real and lived only in SN-006's `why` cell until the safety half moved into that need's normative text at this same pass. OPEN QUESTION FOR THE SITTING, recorded rather than resolved here (2026-08-16 adversarial round, finding F1): with the bound now normative in SN-006, this row's fail-open-on-any-error clause is in tension with that bound — a gate error drops the ceiling the need promises — and the two candidate resolutions (fail closed, or keep failing open under a narrower ceiling that stays enforced when the hook errors) are both behavior changes an owner must rule; the fail-open behavior is UNCHANGED pending that ruling, because a gate that wedges every tool on its own bug is the failure this clause was written against. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
With [checks] subagent_gate = "deny": a Task/Agent spawn is refused (permissionDecision=deny, exit 2); ask defers to approval; off/absent and SUBAGENT_GATE=allow allow it; a non-spawn tool is not gated; a malformed payload fails open (allow) and is logged. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: docs/process.toml [checks] subagent_gate, with the legacy docs/subagent-gate as the migration-window fallback.

SR SR-046current content
SR-ID
SR-046
Title
Declared runnable capabilities reachable from the repository root
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-035
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The launchers at the repository root shall expose every declared runnable capability consistently to an interactive operator, to a direct caller naming one capability, and to automated discovery - from a single declaration, on Windows and POSIX alike.
Rationale
RE-VOICED 2026-08-17 (Sol F14, applied): the shall was a MENU SPECIFICATION - numbered interactive menu, direct named launch, machine listing, empty-declaration text, exit-code passthrough, and the name/name.desc declaration grammar - things that fail independently, which is a decomposition wearing a requirement's clothes. It now states the one capability-level decision: every declared capability is reachable, the same way, from one declaration, by three kinds of caller. Numbering, listing format, declaration grammar, fallback text and exit handling are LLR-047's and the acceptance's. AcceptanceCriteria

SR-046
Title
Run capability menu reader
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/run_menu.py
CodeSymbol
load_capabilities/interactive_menu/direct/launch
Detail
load_capabilities parses the docs/stack.ini [run] section (configparser, interpolation=None, case-preserving optionxform; declaration order kept; a name.desc row attaches to its command and an orphan or empty-value command is dropped) into an ordered (name, command, desc) list; --list prints name<TAB>desc per row; direct(name) launches by exact-match name (exit 2 on an unknown name) and launch() runs the shell line via subprocess shell=True, passing the child exit code straight through; interactive_menu prints the numbered menu, reads a pick from stdin, and returns EOF-safe without hanging; an absent or empty [run] prints guidance and exits 1. The run.* launchers are thin delegates forwarding their args to run_menu.py.
Rationale
The command being run is the user's own declared line, so shell=True is the same trust boundary the declaration already establishes rather than a new one. EOF-safety is load-bearing: a menu that blocks on a closed stdin turns an unattended invocation into a hang, which is the failure this launcher exists to avoid.

Title
Derived gate from artifact states

Boundary-Refs

Requirement
The delivered harness shall derive the gate the project must next pass from the spine artifact states alone, caching it as a derived value — never accepting a hand-set one — with a post-attestation amendment dropping the derived gate exactly as a newly introduced draft does, and the pending-amendment count carried beside the draft count so the pending state never hides.
Rationale
SSOT applied to the gate itself (docs/archive/specs/derived-gate-model.2026-07-20.md): a hand-set marker can be bumped without the work; deriving it makes gate advancement mechanical (SN-004) and un-gameable (SN-008). The hybrid cache keeps the value readable on checkout; --check guards rot. SN-029 JOINED 2026-08-15 (second top-down read, cross-reference repair): the need's acceptance says in its own words that "an AMENDED requirement drops the derived stage exactly as a newly introduced one does", and this row's shall is the only place in the SR layer that states it — the join was simply unrecorded, so a reader asking which rows realize SN-029 got SR-139 (which tiers are held), SR-140 (what an acceptance record contains) and SR-148 (loop selection), none of which carries the drop clause. SR-Refs
SR-049
Title
Generated owner decision surface (registry + attestation depth)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_open_items.py
CodeSymbol
render/_attestation_cards/word_diff/mask_local
Detail
The RENDERED half of the Modified/Draft attestation regime whose gate SR-049 derives. gen_open_items renders (a) every pending row of docs/requirements/open-items.toml as a decision brief and (b) every SR whose Status is Draft (ratification owed) or Modified (re-attest owed) with its whole chain's per-cell before/after. Two contracts the emitted file carries: the attestation-baseline STAMP (a --since render must be reproducible by the freshness gate, or --check compares against a different history) and the check-the-baseline empty state (an auto-derived baseline sitting AFTER a pre-regime amendment renders no cells, which must never read as nothing-changed). The machine-local advisory region is masked before comparing. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: this module RENDERS and owns no second opinion — the attestation model is trace.reattest_model and the pending pointers are gen_trajectory.pending_block, both imported, so a disagreement with `trace.py --ratify` means this view is the defect and the brief wins. NOT claimed: that the rendered page is legible or well-composed.
Rationale
A human cannot bless a delta they cannot see, and the depth that makes a re-attest readable is a WORD-LEVEL diff — which the markdown surface this replaces could not mark, so a reviewer was asked to sign a change they could only read as a whole cell. Re-deriving the attestation model here was rejected: a second opinion that disagreed with the brief would leave the reviewer arbitrating between two surfaces. Both emitted contracts exist because their absence fails SILENTLY in front of someone about to attest, which is the worst place for a quiet failure.

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-052current content
SR-ID
SR-052
Title
Dashboard accessibility (rubric-adjudicated)
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall be operable and readable without a pointer or full color perception: every interactive element (tabs, expandable blocks, SVG nodes with detail panels) is keyboard-reachable and carries an accessible name (title/aria/visible text); no information is encoded by color alone (status/phase/type encodings carry a text or shape cue); text keeps readable contrast against its fill.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — an accessibility bar left unstated silently reads as no bar at all, so the obligation is stated here even though its clauses were not measurable when the row was written. They since became so, one clause at a time, which is why this row's method is mechanical: the thresholds are the child rows' and the acceptance is their chain. Hat-derived (hat.ACCESSIBILITY): keyboard operability, an accessible name per element, colour never the only channel, and a readable contrast floor are the charter's own subject, and no other lens in the roster names a measurable perceptual threshold — the UX pair reaches robustness (it does not overflow), never legibility. That dependency is now CLOSED: the ACCESSIBILITY charter was ruled `always` by the owner on 2026-08-16, so the lens this row derives from is put to every decomposition — and it had already reached this row once SN-023 carried the `a11y` tag, which is why the gap was narrower than the label first recorded. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
Acceptance is the row's decomposed chain, passing: each clause of the requirement — keyboard reachability, an accessible name per interactive element, no information carried by colour alone, and a text-contrast floor — is bound to a child design row whose test case names the automated test holding it, and that binding set is closed over the clauses, so none of them rides an unbound residue. The thresholds, the sweep bounds and the artifact each test reads are the child rows' cells, not restated here.

SR-Refs
SR-052
Title
Non-pruning SVG container roles (A2 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_svg_role/_FOCUSABLE
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): no emitted <svg> declares the CHILDREN-PRESENTATIONAL role=img over a FOCUSABLE descendant, and any svg that does declare role=img carries its own accessible name (aria-label, or a first-direct-child <title> — per SVG-AAM a <title> nested in a <g> names the <g>, not the svg). Focusable means tabindex in any quoting OR a native SVG <a href>, which is tab-ordered without a tabindex. The role is chosen from the emitted BODY by _svg_role. SCOPE — and no wider: the container-role invariant as PROVEN by TC-104, over the artifact this repo ships plus the tiered fixture plus the predicate's own unit cases; it is NOT a proof over every reachable emitter configuration, so an emitter no rendered artifact exercises is unguarded. Name QUALITY belongs to LLR-108/TC-113.
Rationale
role=img prunes its subtree from the accessibility tree, so declaring it over focusable descendants hides every one of them from assistive technology while the page still looks correct. The predicate must treat a native SVG <a href> as focusable and not tabindex alone: a tabindex-only predicate passes a diagram of linked stage cards that are tab-ordered without one. Setting the role at each call site was rejected — deriving it from the body is what stops a future emitter silently reintroducing the conflict.

(see TC-104)

SR-Refs
SR-052
Title
Accessible-name quality, not merely presence (A2 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
_render_drill breadcrumb label; every focusable/role=img emit site
Detail
The QUALITY half of accessibility anchor A2, mechanizing what LLR-101 leaves perceptual. Three rules over every focusable element and role=img graphic: it HAS an accessible name; that name is never a bare registry id; and navigation LANDMARKS are distinct. SCOPE, narrower than a first measurement suggests: A2 governs INTERACTIVE elements and meaningful graphics — a <title> on a decorative edge path is a tooltip, not an accessible name. Uniqueness is asserted for landmarks ONLY: a descend control for the same container legitimately recurs across drill layers, one visible at a time, so those repeats are one control reached by different paths. Whether a well-formed, unique, descriptive name is the RIGHT description belongs to SR-054's reading-task critique.
Rationale
A bare registry id tells a screen-reader user nothing about what the control is, and a reader listing landmarks hears a flat list in which two called 'Breadcrumb' are indistinguishable — which is what three drills produce unless the names are made distinct. Asserting over decorative graphics too was rejected: it would manufacture findings the accessibility standard does not make, and a check that cries wolf is one a reader stops reading.

LLR LLR-112current content
LLR-ID
LLR-112
SR-Refs
SR-052
Title
Keyboard reachability of every wired control (A1 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
emitted querySelectorAll wiring; tabindex + native-link emission
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-accessibility.md): every element the emitted page WIRES an interaction to is keyboard-focusable. The wired set is DERIVED from the document's own DOM-selecting calls and then classified — a control selector (its matches receive per-element click/focus/dblclick/keydown listeners) may match only elements carrying tabindex=0 or natively tab-ordered (a button; an SVG a-with-href, the LLR-101 predicate), and each non-control selector records why its matches are not focus stops. A newly wired selector fails the owning test until classified. The derivation reads EVERY DOM-selecting API (querySelector/querySelectorAll/closest/matches, any quote style) and fails loudly on a non-literal selector argument, which a static read cannot classify. A control-selector wiring loop that attaches click or dblclick must attach focus or keydown beside it, statically asserted. Order-integrity: document order IS emission order, no positive tabindex may reorder the walk, and tabindex=-1 is legal only on the roving tablist buttons. SCOPE — and no wider: focusability, order-integrity and activation PAIRING of the wired set over the shipped artifact plus the seven-fixture emitter sweep. Runtime behaviour equivalence is not driven, and whether the order FEELS sensible stays a reader-experience judgement. Runtime-created controls (the breadcrumb buttons) are native <button>s, keyboard-operable by construction and outside the static walk — a recorded limit.
Rationale
A control reachable only by pointer is unreachable for a keyboard user, and the page still looks complete, so nothing reports it. Enumerating the controls by hand was rejected: a per-element list goes stale the moment a selector is added, whereas deriving the wired set from the document's own calls means a new selector fails until someone classifies it. Two narrowings are load-bearing and must not be relaxed back: the derivation has to read every DOM-selecting API rather than one spelling of one call, or it silently misses wired controls; and the key-handler half has to be asserted statically rather than deferred to a browser, or mouse-only wiring ships.
TestRefs
(see TC-117)

Approved

SR-052
Title
No information by colour alone (A3 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
tier_legend + sw/phase/status legend emitters; STATUS_GLYPH
Detail
The mechanized core of accessibility anchor A3: for every declared colour vocabulary (enumerated from the module constants, the U5/U2 closure, so a new vocabulary enters by existing), every member that actually PAINTS in a rendered document resolves to a legend swatch labelled in words in that same document, with CSS tokens resolved. Status members additionally pair a per-status glyph. The emitted JS detail-badge maps are SUBSTITUTED from TIER_FILL/STATUS_FILL rather than hand-copied, and three drift guards hold every copy equal to the constants: the emitted JS maps byte-match the Python palettes; the CSS status tokens match STATUS_FILL and are asserted theme-invariant; and the tier-legend bijection derives its expectation from the TIER_FILL dict, so a member dropped from the legend also fails. SCOPE and its narrowing: the worded cue must exist in the same document — that it sits within eyeshot of the painted element is layout this does not re-assert. The JS detail-panel badge stays out of the LEGEND rule because its background carries its concept as the badge's own visible text.
Rationale
Colour alone excludes any reader who cannot resolve it, and the failure is silent: the page looks informative to everyone who can. A raw hex scan was rejected as the detection method because tokenised fills under-report it — the status legend paints through a CSS variable, and a hex scan finds one member of four. The substitution and the three guards exist because a hand-copied palette copy is the recurring defect here: one stale hex shipped in three separate places, and each copy that a test shares with its emitter is a copy the test cannot catch. The same-document narrowing costs a reader nothing today (every painted member is cued in its own tab panel) and a re-attestation should bless it knowingly.

Title
Text-contrast floors, closed over the vocabulary set (A4)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS + _ring_ink
Detail
Accessibility anchor A4 bound. The WCAG 2.1 AA arithmetic is owned by six sibling tests — node fills vs label ink at 4.5:1, the emitted badge/boundary pairs, the no-opacity-discount rule, theme-token fills behind white text in BOTH themes, the hub-is-not-accent regression, and the per-fill focus ring at the 3:1 boundary floor — so this row adds the BINDING plus the gap none of them owned: the SET. Closed by reflection: every UPPERCASE module-level collection holding hex members must be one of the five declared vocabularies, and every member of the REFLECTED set clears 4.5:1 against its label ink (white, except the one declared queued dark-ink exception) and 3:1 for its computed focus ring. The walk is RECURSIVE (nested dicts, tuples, sets, bare strings), counts any 3/4/6/8-digit hex form as hex-bearing, and requires every member be CANONICAL 6-digit lowercase. The theme-token half is DERIVED from the emitted CSS: every fill:var(--x) token must carry a declared role, white-text-fill roles clear 4.5:1 under white in BOTH themes, container-fill roles keep --text readable in both. SCOPE — and no wider: contrast arithmetic over declared constants and emitted theme tokens (the ring boundary also binds under SR-054 T5 as LLR-105); a lowercase module-level palette would evade the reflection and is forbidden by the module's UPPERCASE-constant convention, a recorded reliance. Rendered-pixel measurement is not asserted.
Rationale
Each sibling test sweeps a hand-copied list of the palettes, so a sixth vocabulary could ship outside every floor check while all six stayed green — the arithmetic was right and the SET was the hole. A new vocabulary must enter the arithmetic by EXISTING rather than by being remembered, which is what reflection buys and a seventh hand-copied list would not. Two shapes of that same defect must not return: a flat-dict-only walk misses nested and non-dict collections, and a hand-listed theme-token set is the very defect this row claims to close — both were live here, which is why the walk is recursive and the token set derived.

Title

SN-Refs

Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall read as one system across its tabs and views: one type scale and spacing rhythm; one status/phase/type color vocabulary applied consistently wherever the same concept renders; uniform node, edge, legend, and detail-panel styling across the SVG emitters; the same interaction idiom (expand, hover, detail) wherever the same structure appears.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — how alike is alike enough is subjective at the margins, which is why this row states the coherence property and lets each clause of it be pinned separately rather than pinning the whole to one threshold and pinning the wrong thing. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is the anchor census of the uniformity bar as it was first written — five anchors, three of them leaving a mechanized residue — not a decision count; this row states one cross-view coherence property, and every child exists because an anchor was bound to a test rather than because a second contract was merged in. STATED HONESTLY RATHER THAN CLAIMED: the charter did not exist when this row was written — three independent derivations (needs-only, frame-only, hat-aware) each failed to produce this obligation, which is why it was the one row the exercise could call underivable rather than merely underived — and it was drafted at this pass (2026-08-16, finding R-4) to close that gap. The owner RULED the charter in on 2026-08-16, so this row has a deriving lens for the first time and the disposition question it carried is settled. The narrower reading survives the ruling and is the one to re-open if the charter is ever cut: no stakeholder need states cross-view coherence, so the lens — not a need — is what reaches this row. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16; charter ruled, row retained.)

Title

Title
Process-tab node-label type scale (U1 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
--nhead/.stgt/.stgn/.hooplab/.hubname
Detail
The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md): the Process tab's working-loops SVG no longer hardcodes 12px/9.5px/13px/13px on .stgt/.stgn/.hooplab/.hubname. .stgt/.stgn are the same per-node-label role as the shared --nlabel/--nsub tokens the icicle, dag and knowledge views already share, and reuse them directly; .hooplab/.hubname are a once-per-diagram headline label, a genuinely different role, so they take the one documented --nhead scale step. SCOPE: these four selectors resolve to a declared token, asserted structurally against the emitted CSS. Whether the sizes read as uniform belongs to LLR-111/TC-116.
Rationale
A magic number is invisible to every later change: two independently-drifting literals doing one job diverge the first time somebody adjusts one of them. Collapsing all four selectors onto the node-label tokens was rejected because a once-per-diagram headline is a different role, and forcing one token onto two roles is the same defect in the other direction.

SR-Refs
SR-053
Title
Single-sourced concept colour vocabulary (U2 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
SW_NODE_FILL/STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
Detail
The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U2 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Every concept's fill is LOOKED UP from a declared module-level palette, so the same concept cannot render two hexes — there is only one place a hex can come from. Concretely: every #rrggbb appearing in a rendered dashboard is either a member of a declared palette collection or a `--token:#hex` theme definition (surfaces, text and borders, which encode no concept). SCOPE: this proves single-sourcing of the VOCABULARY, not that a concept was assigned a well-chosen hue — collision and perceptual separation belong to LLR-102.
Rationale
A vocabulary is one vocabulary only if every member is in it: a bare hex literal outside the declared collection is invisible to the collision sweep and to the ring-ink enumeration alike, so it evades both the checks that would catch it. That is not hypothetical — a four-kind vocabulary declared only three because one badge fill sat inline in its emitter. Auditing hues by review was rejected: review cannot see a member that was never declared.

(see TC-109)

SR-053
Title
One interaction idiom per node role (U4 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_views.py
CodeSymbol
_drill_layer_svg/arch_icicle/dag_svg/know_graph_svg node groups
Detail
The mechanized core of uniformity anchor U4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-uniformity.md). Two node roles exist and each is spelled ONE way in every emitter that renders it: an IDENTIFIED node (icicle .cell, flat-DAG .wi, knowledge .knode) advertises its registry id through `data-id`; a DETAIL-BEARING drill node (.block, in both the When and How-SW drills) advertises its detail-map key through `data-node`; and a node of either role is made focusable the same way, `tabindex="0"` on the group. Neither role ever carries the other's attribute. SCOPE: ATTRIBUTE parity, not runtime behaviour parity.
Rationale
One node addressed two ways means every later consumer must handle both, and the second spelling is the one that gets forgotten. Proving that two emitters' handlers BEHAVE alike was considered and rejected as out of reach without a browser harness — what is provable from the markup is that they are wired through one vocabulary, and that is the narrowing this row accepts explicitly rather than claiming more than it checks.

SR-Refs
SR-053
Title
Pairwise deltaE floor across every vocabulary (U5 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-102 leaves perceptual: a PAIRWISE deltaE floor across every declared vocabulary, not only within PHASE_ACCENTS. Two floors, each a recorded judgement — 15 WITHIN a vocabulary (every member can sit beside every other in one legend) and 12 ACROSS (two vocabularies share no legend, so the floor is lower rather than absent). The declared tier<->okf mirror is exempt BY DESIGN and is asserted BYTE-EQUAL, so 'exempt' cannot drift into 'nearly equal'. SCOPE: perceptual SEPARATION of the declared palettes. Whether a given hue suits its concept is not asserted.
Rationale
'A collision the identity check misses' looked like a judgement call only because the formula was already there and nobody had widened the set it ran over — the same arithmetic on more members. A cross-vocabulary floor of zero was rejected on evidence: a reader conflated a pair drawn from two different vocabularies, so sharing no legend reduces the risk without removing it. Three live conflations were sitting in the shipped palettes at the binding — sw external/component at deltaE 8.6, SR/phase[3] at 9.5, Interface/phase[7] at 10.5.

SR-Refs
SR-053
Title
Declared weight/alpha/corner tokens (U3 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
--w-*/--o-*/--r-* tokens; SVG_RX
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-103 leaves perceptual. Fourteen CSS tokens plus SVG_RX name one step per role for stroke-width, opacity and corner radius, near-duplicates merged into the role's step, and every emitted value must resolve to a declared token. stroke-opacity is checked under the opacity scale deliberately — it is the same scale applied to a stroke. SVG_RX is enforced against both the source literals and every render, because the rx presentation attribute cannot read a CSS custom property portably. SCOPE: token membership, asserted over paint surfaces (style blocks and style/fill/stroke attributes), never over prose that quotes CSS.
Rationale
'Spacing and exact visual weight' was undecidable because the weights were never declared: the shipped page carried 8 stroke-widths, 7 opacities, 5 corner radii and 6 SVG rx values, of which five stroke widths were doing the single job 'draw a connector' and four rx values 'round a node box'. Nothing can be uniform against an undeclared scale, so declaring the roles is what turns the judgement into a check; leaving it to review was rejected because a near-duplicate weight is exactly what an eye does not catch.

Title
Declared type scale, no raw font-size literals (U1 residue)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
--nlabel/--nsub/--nhead + --tiny/--xsmall/--small/--body/--lead/--display/--hero + --rel
Detail
Mechanizes what LLR-104 left perceptual. 'Do the sizes read as visually uniform' was undecidable because the scale was never written down: 18 raw font-size literals against 5 tokens, with .7/.75rem, .9/.95/.98rem, 1.05/1.1rem and 8.5/9px each being near-duplicate steps for ONE role 3-7% apart. Eleven steps are now declared in THREE documented families - node (px, because SVG geometry is fixed px and a rem resizes labels out of their boxes), page (rem), and one relative step (em, for text sized against its parent) - and every emitted font-size must resolve to one. Claiming a single scale across those units would be false, so the LLR states three. SCOPE: step membership over paint surfaces. Whether the chosen steps are well-proportioned is not asserted.

SR-054Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)re-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-054current content
SR-ID
SR-054
Title
Dashboard usability (rubric-adjudicated)
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The state view the delivered generators produce shall support its core reading tasks with low friction: a first-time reviewer finds the project state, the next work, and how the parts connect, each within one tab switch; views default to a legible density (start-collapsed per the greater-than-3 rule) and reveal detail without losing context; labels stay readable at default zoom with no clipped or overlapping text.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 and SN-023 — task-level usability is perceptual (is this findable, is this legible), so a test can confirm an element exists and not that a reader can use it. The bar is therefore written from the stakeholder intent rather than from the test, because a test written by the builder encodes the builder's own idea of usable. Most of it turned out to be pinnable and is now held mechanically; one clause about a first-time reader's experience is not, and it rests on a recorded one-time judgement rather than on a standing re-judgement — the residue named at the child, and stated there as a limit rather than implied as coverage. Fan-out re-stamp: the child count is that same anchor census, not a decision count — this row states one perceptual property, and each anchor pinned to a test as it was mechanized left an LLR behind, so the fan-out rises as the row is mechanized. Hat-derived (hat.UX-DESIGNER + hat.UX-ENGINEER): findability is the designer's — a declared reader and the decision answered without hunting (C-UXD-1) — and legibility-as-robustness is the engineer's — real widths and real volumes, nothing clipped or overlapping (C-UXE-2). Both are `always` in this repo, so unlike its two siblings this row's deriving lens is reachable today. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
Acceptance is the row's decomposed chain, passing: each clause of the requirement a test can hold — a labelled entry point per core reading task, the start-collapsed density default, the return path that reveals detail without losing the parent, and label legibility with nothing clipped, overflowing its own box, or crossing an unrelated one — is bound to a child design row whose test case names the automated test holding it, and that binding set is closed over the mechanized clauses. The clause no test holds is carried by the row's one non-automated child, whose recorded judgement is its evidence; the instrument, the live-versus-retired anchor set and the artifact recipe are that child's cells, not restated here.
Priority
C
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
3

Byte-deterministic; --check unchanged. This row keeps ONLY what a test cannot pin: the mechanized cores have left for child rows that name the tests owning them — T2 start-collapse (LLR-099/TC-102), T3 breadcrumb-restores-parent (LLR-100/TC-103, the whole anchor, no residue here) and T1 labelled entry (LLR-115/TC-120, the whole anchor, no residue here). Do NOT restate a child's claim here: an anchor lives in exactly one row.
Rationale
A critique that re-judges what a test already enforces spends its budget twice and can disagree with the harness, so an anchor whose bar becomes operational leaves this row entirely rather than being kept as a duplicate. T3 and T1 are the two that left whole: a critique cannot verify T3's return path from a static shot set while a test can click the crumb, and T1's 'obvious' was always glossed as labelled-not-unlabelled, which is mechanizable once written that way.

Title
Detail-in-context breadcrumb (T3 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol

Detail

Title
Interactive-control contrast in both themes (T5 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_ring_ink/_ring_style/_cedge_marker
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T5 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md): EVERY control painted over a node's own fill clears the WCAG 3:1 UI-boundary floor against EVERY node fill declared in STATUS_FILL/TIER_FILL/OKF_TYPE_FILL/SW_NODE_FILL/PHASE_ACCENTS, in BOTH themes — computed per fill (white or near-black, whichever contrasts more), never a single fixed hue. Fixing only the ring was rejected once the same fixed token was found on the descend ARROW — the very control a reader must find to descend a tier — measuring 1.06:1 light and 1.99:1 dark on a shipped phase fill. One control's fix does not generalise; deriving every such control from the host fill does.

Title
Task findability - labelled entry within one tab switch (T1 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
CodeSymbol
build_html/_next_work_html/_nav
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T1 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Each of the three core reading tasks the rubric concretizes — find the project state, find the next work, find how the parts connect — is reachable within one tab switch of the landing view, its entry point a LABELLED nav control or a NAMED surface on the landing view, never an unlabelled region a reader must discover by clicking. THE DESIGN CONSTRAINT that makes the check honest: it is judged against the RENDERED artifact carrying real registry data, NEVER the nav skeleton. The guard resolves each task's entry point from the emitted document (task 1 -> the landing Definition-completeness/Execution cards, 0 switches; task 2 -> the landing Next-work card, 0 switches; task 3 -> the How-SW nav tab, 1 switch), asserts each is LABELLED and within one switch, and asserts the next-work surface is NON-EMPTY on the zero-active/all-queued fixture. The rendered-artifact constraint is the load-bearing part and must not be relaxed to a structural check: a next-work surface can EXIST in the emitter and render nothing when no row is active, so a nav-skeleton check passes on exactly the artifact a reader finds empty.

Title
Viewport-fit by scale-to-fit with a legibility floor (T7 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py
CodeSymbol
_svg_fit_style/SHRINK_FLOOR
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T7 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). Every emitted SVG (all three wrappers: _svg_wrap for dag/sw/know, the icicle panel, _drill_layer_svg) carries width:100% + max-width:<natural> + min-width:<natural x SHRINK_FLOOR> instead of a FIXED pixel width, so a diagram fills its container and scales down as the viewport narrows. A viewBox alone cannot fix it — the fixed width still wins. Title
Theme-lock: one theme mechanism and no mixed-family surface/ink pair (T6 core)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
HTML_TEMPLATE theme tokens + every emitter's paint
Detail
The mechanized core of usability anchor T6 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). The measurable content is FAMILY PAIRING, not the absence of literals: a fixed status swatch carrying fixed white ink is theme-locked CORRECTLY, since neither side flips. A page inverts mid-view in exactly two ways and this row forbids both. (1) A SECOND THEME MECHANISM below :root — the document declares exactly one prefers-color-scheme block, targeting :root, and one color-scheme, also at :root. (2) A MIXED PAIR — a theme-varying ink on a theme-invariant surface, or the reverse. The two families are DERIVED from the emitted document (the dark block's override set IS the definition of theme-varying), never hand-listed, and the dark block may not override a token :root never declares. Applied to CSS rules declaring both a background and an ink, to every emitted SVG node pairing an inline rect fill with an inline text fill, and to CSS-declared SVG text fills paired against the family their host nodes actually paint in. SCOPE and its deliberate narrowings: --ring is excluded because it is stamped FROM the host fill (LLR-105 owns its floor); the JS-composed badge idiom pairs background and ink at runtime from the invariant vocabulary, where LLR-114's arithmetic owns it; and the page CSS has exactly ONE rule declaring a classifiable background AND ink today (body), so that half is prospective while the sweep's weight sits on the node and text-fill halves. Whether the chosen palettes are PLEASANT stays perceptual.
Rationale
A per-component theme override is a seam the reader crosses while scrolling, and a mixed pair reads correctly in the theme it was authored in and inverts in the other — both are invisible to the author working in one theme. Forbidding colour LITERALS was the obvious rule and is the wrong one: it fails a correct fixed-on-fixed pairing and passes a varying-on-invariant one, so it would flag the safe case and miss the defect. Deriving the families from the emitted document rather than listing them means a new token joins a family by existing.

(see TC-122)

SR-054
Title
T4 label legibility, mechanized: ink inside its box, truncation with a reveal
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_trajectory.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_render.py;project-trajectory/scripts/traj_panels.py
CodeSymbol
_drill_block_label/_fit_lines/_next_work_title
Detail
The mechanized floor of usability anchor T4 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md), widening the anchor's existing owner rather than adding a rubric anchor. The clause made objective: no character a block renders may fall outside that block's own rect, horizontally or vertically. The emitter FITS the sub-label to the column it will actually be drawn in — a second line when one will not hold it, then an ellipsis — with three text lines the ceiling, reusing the grid the id/name wrap idiom already proved fits row_h; a sub-label that already fits one line renders byte-identically to before. SCOPE — PROVEN: for every block of every drill layer in the emitted document, each label line's ink box lies inside its own node rect, measured from the emitted geometry with the emitter's declared per-character widths and the document's declared font sizes, entities counted as the single glyph they render. Second half: this emitter does not truncate the next-work title by a fixed budget — the card emits the whole clause and lets its own width do the fitting, the declared bound _NEXT_WORK_TITLE caps ONE item's HEIGHT rather than its text, and where it bites the remainder discloses through a NATIVE <details> cut at a word so head and remainder rejoin continuously when opened. DELIBERATELY NOT: whether an ellipsis a reader meets is ACTIONABLE — truncated-without-affordance stays T4's perceptual residue under LLR-055/TC-055, so T4 remains a live critique anchor; the full string keeps its homes in the block's title, aria-label and data-summary.
Rationale
A label is illegible AS a label the moment its ink leaves the box it names, which is the half of this anchor a reader cannot argue with. Column right-sizing (LLR-057) cannot absorb it: right-sizing clamps at MAX_TIER_COL, so a 400-character need asks for a 2000px column and gets 172 — the overflow is structural, not a tuning problem. A FIXED character budget was the shipped approach on the next-work card and is rejected: it spends the same budget at every width, cutting a title mid-word on a wide screen with the card half empty. Native <details> is the point of the reveal — operable by pointer AND keyboard with no script, in a file opened from disk, which is what makes the affordance assertable from the markup instead of needing a browser. The height bound is measured over 320 registry rows: median 44, p90 126, max 609 characters of leading clause.

Title
No edge through an unrelated node box (T8 objective floor)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/traj_graph.py
CodeSymbol
_route_edges/_detour_d/_polyline_hits
Detail
The mechanized floor of usability anchor T8 (docs/rubrics/dashboard-usability.md). T8 is two clauses and this row claims only the first: NO EDGE PASSES THROUGH AN UNRELATED NODE BOX. SCOPE — PROVEN: across the shipped dashboard plus one fixture per emitter, every emitted wire, sampled as the polyline a reader's eye follows, clears every node rect that is not its own source or target. MEASURED at the binding: 264 shipped wires, 0 through a box. DELIBERATELY NOT: crossing count, lane separation, or where a crossing falls — that residue stays under LLR-055/TC-055, which is why T8 remains a live critique anchor. Separately and NOT claimed here: at the router's own 3px clearance rather than the bare box, one wire does graze, so _detour_d exhausting its lane candidates and returning the least-bad fallback is a reachable state rather than a theoretical one; that wire's identity and the lane-separation work belong to the open work item.
Rationale
A wire crossing an unrelated box reads as a connection that is not there, which is a wrong answer rather than an untidy one — geometry a reader cannot argue with. The claim about the ARTIFACT is deliberately separate from the router's own unit tests and is why this row exists: _detour_d returns a LEAST-BAD fallback when no candidate lane clears, so 'the algorithm detours' does not imply 'nothing shipped through a box', and the artifact-level claim had only ever been hand-verified once.

SR-070Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkablere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-070current content
SR-ID
SR-070
Title
Generated views are offline, deterministic and drift-checkable
SN-Refs
SN-010;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered generator set shall derive each artifact it produces from the tracked registries alone — self-contained and usable without network access, byte-stable across regeneration from unchanged sources, and carrying a freshness contract that fails when the committed copy has drifted from those sources — omitting a view whose source registries the adopting repository does not carry rather than emitting an empty one.
Rationale
Realizes SN-010 and the dissolved edge expectation that a generated artifact drifting from its source fails its --check, by isolating one independently reviewable obligation formerly carried by SR-038; with SN-012, since a repository that carries no source registry pays nothing for the view that registry would have fed. SPLIT 2026-08-16 (re-tier v2 S4, ruling R1 — one decision per row): this row had accreted the SR-census's "natural parent of the generators cluster" role and carried sixteen direct children spanning two unrelated obligations — the INTEGRITY of a generated artifact, and what the state view must SHOW. Those fail independently: a view can be perfectly offline, deterministic and drift-checkable while showing nothing about the work in flight or how the parts connect. The content half sheds along the conjunction SN-023 itself draws ("progress AND how its parts connect") to SR-168 and SR-169; this row keeps the artifact-integrity contract alone. The subject is "each artifact the generator set produces" rather than "each view" deliberately: the knowledge-export bundle is not a view, and a fourth row minted to hold it would be the filler tier the ruling forbids. The child/parent phase spread the 2026-08-14 ledger recorded as INTENDED is untouched — this split is by observable class, not by phase, which that ledger considered and rejected.
AcceptanceCriteria
Regeneration from unchanged sources is byte-identical; a generated artifact opens and stays usable with no network access and no external fetch; a committed copy that has drifted from its sources fails its freshness contract while a current one passes; a repository lacking a source registry omits that view and stays byte-stable rather than emitting an empty one. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: gen_trajectory.py --check, gen_arch_map.py --check, gen_okf.py.

SR-137One policy home, with a checked shapere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-137current content
SR-ID
SR-137
Title
One policy home, with a checked shape
SN-Refs
SN-028
Boundary-Refs
B-01;B-04
Requirement
The kit shall read every process policy dial from a single policy declaration home whose line shape is a checked contract (one key = value per line under a bare [section] header; no dotted keys, inline tables or multi-line strings), refusing — never resolving by precedence — a repo in which any dial is declared both there and in its legacy one-word file.
Rationale
Two grammars read this file — `tomllib` in Python and the three git hooks in pure sh, so a Python-less box still fails closed on a declared privacy gate (M-42). TOML is far more expressive than a `grep -E` can follow, so every shape only one reader understands is a silent flip of a security gate; the first cut shipped five such shapes, three of them fail-OPEN. Narrowing the shape and CHECKING the narrowing is what makes one file safe for two readers. ONE HOME, settled 2026-08-15: the two-grammars-agree observable — the same answer from the hooks' sh and `tomllib` over the adversarial file-shape table — is SR-031's and SR-031's alone. Both rows claimed it and the texts had already DIVERGED, only SR-031's naming the trailing-comment decoy that once made the privacy gate fail OPEN, which is the whole reason the table exists. The partition that survives: SR-031 states the package-wide coherence property (every enforcer reads one value per dial, both grammars agreeing) at B-05; this row states the dial's HOME and its CHECKED SHAPE, and the refusals a session gets back, at B-01/B-04. What stays here is the shape check's own fail-safe direction — a shape only one reader understands is refused AND still reads ON in both — because that is a property of the narrowing, not of the comparison. CROSSING ATTRIBUTION EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED 2026-08-15 (the act-7 revision ["B-01","B-02"] -> ["B-01","B-04"], applied and flagged for overrule): read against external.toml, B-02 carries exactly three things - rulings, attestations and Status flips - and this row's shall contains none of them. Its subject is the dial file's HOME and SHAPE, so its observables are the config edit arriving as a governed write through the hook floor (B-01) and the refusal going back out naming the key and both files (B-04). The contrary reading - that declaring a policy dial is itself an authority act - was weighed and REJECTED on the partition it would destroy: the dial's MEANING is SR-139's and that row keeps B-02, so putting B-02 here would give one authority observable two homes, which is the defect this campaign exists to remove. One honest strain is recorded rather than hidden: B-04's `carries` names the hook floor and subagent_gate specifically, while this row's refusals also fire at the dispatcher preflight, intake's adjudication arm and the integrator's verdict gate - session-facing guardrail verdicts of the same class, but not the two the crossing enumerates. If that enumeration is meant as exhaustive, B-04's own text is what needs widening, not this row's attribution.
AcceptanceCriteria
A dial declared in both homes produces a refusal naming the key and both files, at every guarded entry point (the dispatcher's pre-claim preflight, intake's adjudication arm, the integrator's verdict gate); a dotted key, inline table, multi-line string or hook-read key under the wrong section is refused by the shape check AND still reads ON in both readers; a wrong-typed or out-of-range dial is refused rather than defaulted. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: docs/process.toml, with the legacy one-word files as the refused second home.

SR-144Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable recordre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-144current content
SR-ID
SR-144
Title
Every lane close is a terminal state with an immutable record
SN-Refs
SN-027
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch, with one immutable per-close report naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split — refusing a report that declares neither a split nor an explicit deferral, and refusing a second close of the same event rather than overwriting the first.
Rationale
Five successive dedup mechanisms leaked because each reconstructed the return event from a MUTABLE proxy; a document that never moves dissolves the class instead of mitigating it. The refusals are what make "immutable" a property rather than a claim. The split is a field because its absence merged rejected code onto trunk on 2026-08-03: silence about it is different from saying "I could not judge", and only silence is unactionable. Hat-derived (hat.UNATTENDED-OPS): a claim a dead lane never releases is C-UNA-3's deadlocked-until-morning case, and the immutable per-close record is C-UNA-5's answer to a failure that pages nobody — a close no one can read is externally indistinguishable from a run that finished. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
A partial close is clean under the real validator (the terminal Deliverable rule is exempt, because an early close leaves the spec's definition byte-identical by design); a terminal spec with no report is LOUD rather than silently un-disposed; a second close is refused and leaves no residue in the lane; a multi-spec close that refuses restores every report it wrote.

S
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
5

LLR-ID
LLR-144
SR-Refs
SR-144
Title
Terminal lane outcomes (handback + quarantine)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/quarantine/report_path/render_report/_restore/diff_records
Detail
close_partial: commits the lane's work as-is, moves each claimed spec to the TERMINAL partial/ (nothing re-claims it, so nothing strands) and writes ONE IMMUTABLE per-close report under docs/handbacks/ naming the claimed outcome, the reason, the commit range and the keep/discard split. The report is the close EVENT's identity — immutable so it never moves, unique so a second close is a second document — which is what dissolved the starvation class five mutable-proxy dedup mechanisms died on. A second close is refused rather than overwriting the first, and any refusal restores every report the call wrote (schedule reads queued+blockref as blocked) and the driver cannot claim and return it forever. quarantine: the ruled red arm - reverts the product paths to the merge base and keeps the failing diff as a bar-inert .patch under docs/work/handback/, reading `git diff --name-status -z` as three-field RECORDS so a rename cannot revert the wrong path, checking every git return code, and restoring rather than reporting when a revert step fails. Both leave the branch finished, so integrate.branch_outcomes reads the outcome off the spec's DIRECTORY - complete/=merged, cancelled/=cancelled, any open folder=handback - and every lane ends in a merge.
Rationale
Making the outcome the directory leaves no fourth answer and no state file that could hold one; partial work lands in trunk where a future WI can find it instead of on a branch nobody will look at.

SR-144
Title
The close ritual and the disposition it owes
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/handback.py
CodeSymbol
close_partial/render_report/report_path/_restore/_split_lines
Detail
`close_partial` writes the report FIRST (it is the event's identity), refuses when one already exists, and restores EVERY report this call wrote on any refusal — so a multi-spec lane cannot half-close. `intake._close_drafts` titles the disposition on the report PATH and nothing else, and says so LOUDLY when a terminal spec has no report rather than minting zero rows quietly. Every partial and cancelled close earns exactly one disposition row — a re-sweep mints none, a genuinely second close is a second report and so a second row, and a disposition row never mints a disposition for itself (refused at both ends); a complete close is spot-checked at the declared rate rather than gated; continuing partial work drafts a successor carrying explicit lineage that survives the id change, never re-opening a terminal row.
Rationale
The path is the only token in the event's identity that cannot move. An earlier cut folded the report's `claimed_outcome` in as well, which re-read a MUTABLE field on every sweep, so one edit minted a second disposition for the same close — the starvation class returning through a new proxy.

Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).
