SR-146Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trailre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-146current content
SR-ID
SR-146
Title
Prompts are reviewable files with a per-session audit trail
SN-Refs
SN-005
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Every prompt the delivered loop launches shall be a shipped, reviewable file with strictly filled slots — listed by digest in a freshness-gated generated catalogue, each session recording which template it used and the fingerprint of what it rendered to.
Rationale
Prose steers the sessions this loop launches and had been reviewable only by reading Python source, which makes the process trusted rather than inspectable. The audit trail is the part that makes the move useful rather than cosmetic: without a per-session digest, "which instruction did this session see" stays an inference. (The argv-vs-shell-string transport decision is design, and lives one tier down in LLR-163.) Hat-derived (hat.SECURITY): C-SEC-5 requires that content composed for dispatch to an external model runner carry a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — a prompt assembled inside source is an unreviewed egress path no write-side gate covers, and shipping it as a reviewable file with a digest is what makes that rule readable. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
An unknown or unfilled slot is a refusal; a missing shipped template is a named preflight refusal rather than a first-session crash; the catalogue lists every shipped prompt with its digest and `--check` fails when stale; a line-ending difference is not a digest change; an operator override is named in the session record rather than only in the launch flags.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
5
Aspect
process
LLR LLR-162current content
LLR-ID
LLR-162
SR-Refs
SR-146
Title
Prompts as loaded files, catalogued and fingerprinted
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/prompts.py
CodeSymbol
load/fill/strict_check/digest/catalog_rows/preflight
Detail
`load` reads a shipped template (or an operator override) and raises a named `PromptError`; `fill` refuses an unknown slot and an unfilled declared slot; `digest` normalizes line endings before hashing; `preflight` turns a missing template into a launch-time refusal. `gen_prompt_catalog.py` renders the catalogue with a `--check` freshness gate, and `agent_common.prompt_fingerprint` records the rendered result per session beside the template's own name.
Rationale
Normalizing before hashing is not cosmetic: the same template checked out CRLF on Windows and LF on POSIX is the same prompt, and a telemetry field that said otherwise would report a model change on every clone.

SR-Refs
SR-146
Title
Argv arrays, and the adjudicator as a routed phase
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_session.py
CodeSymbol
split_cmd/build_argv
Detail
`split_cmd` recognises a JSON array ONLY when the value parses as a list, so no existing template can change meaning; a non-string token raises rather than being stringified. The prompt-transport decision reads the resulting TOKENS, so it is identical for both forms. Alongside it, `ADJUDICATE` joins `agent_route.WEIGHT_PHASES` and `agent_loop.DEFAULT_PHASE_TIER` with its own `route_intent` arm: cross-family like the reviewers, tier pinned by the row's measured estimate, and deliberately deaf to the implementer's escalation overrides.
Rationale
A silent coercion in a registry is a launch that does not match its declaration. And an adjudicator inheriting the implementer's escalation would silently re-tier a judgement because a different row's build went badly.

SR-146
Title
The generated prompt catalogue and its freshness gate
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/gen_prompt_catalog.py
CodeSymbol
render/main
Detail
`render` is PURE — it takes no arguments and reads only the shipped templates — so `--check` and the write path cannot disagree about what "current" means. `--check` compares on NORMALIZED line endings, for the same reason the digest is taken that way: a CRLF checkout is not a stale catalogue. The file is declared in `docs/stack.ini [generated]` as kind `promptcatalog`, so the trunk lane owns it and a work branch never commits it.
Rationale
A generated artifact nobody re-generates is a document that lies, and a lying catalogue is worse than none: it invites the operator to conclude the wrong thing about the session they are debugging. Same contract `gen_arch_map --check` and `gen_skills_index --check` hold.

SR-147One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spinere-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-147current content
SR-ID
SR-147
Title
One machine-parseable carrier for the requirement spine
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall hold every spine tier in one machine-parseable representation, reached by a migration proven cell-for-cell over the live registries before the new representation becomes authoritative.
Rationale
RE-VOICED 2026-08-17 (Sol F16, applied): the shall carried the migration HISTORY - naming the two prior carriers and the 2026-08-10 cutover - plus a second obligation, which is normative history and a merged decision at the requirement tier. The history lives here now, where a rationale may hold it, and the row states one obligation. The recorded 13v waiver covered the one-shall finding ONLY and is SPENT: the row no longer carries a second shall and trace.py no longer reports it. The two-carrier split has no recorded rationale and costs on both sides. Reading 32 need rows takes ~166 code lines across 14 functions in 8 modules - six bespoke scanners, two of them F5 twins pinned by nothing but a docstring, which have already drifted once and rendered a phantom root in the dashboard - while reading all 436 SR+LLR+TC rows takes csv.DictReader. CSV also cannot represent the cells that actually exist: five hold a literal pipe, 552 hold commas, the longest is 1,553 characters, and an embedded newline has no representation at all. A structured carrier turns three integrity rules into properties of the parse - a duplicate id becomes a decode error, a ref list becomes a typed array (retiring the split-on-whitespace rule that read `and` as an orphan), and an empty cell becomes an absent key, so `unset` and `set to empty` stop being the same value. The converter is the migration's evidence, not its convenience: SR-129's 140-cell lesson is that an unproven representation change is exactly where a registry silently loses cells. One-shall waiver (13v): the single-carrier claim and its converter-proof are one contract — the converter is the migration's evidence — and the converter's mechanics already decompose to this row's LLR chain; splitting would separate a claim from the proof that makes it checkable. Hat-derived (hat.TEST-ENGINEER): the single-carrier claim is checkable only because the converter proves it cell-for-cell BEFORE the authority flip — the charter refuses an obligation whose enforcer has never been shown to bite, and a carrier change is precisely where a silent loss hides. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
migrate_carrier.py --check over the live registries exits 0 reporting the round-trip clean for every row of every tier; a cell that is dropped, truncated, re-typed or re-ordered is REFUSED with exit 1 and a finding naming the registry, the row id and the field; and the emitted text re-parses with tomllib.
Priority
C
Verification
Test
Status
Modified
Phase
5
Aspect
trajectory
LLR LLR-165current content
LLR-ID
LLR-165
SR-Refs
SR-147
Title
The spine carrier converter (markdown + CSV -> TOML)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/migrate_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
convert/rows_to_toml/sn_to_toml/compare/toml_scalar
Detail
convert() walks the four tiers and returns (findings, written_paths), so the caller decides whether a lossy conversion writes. rows_to_toml emits `[<table>.<ID>]` with the id PREFIX RETAINED and BARE - TOML allows `-` in a bare key - because ~6,400 hand-authored citations use the prefixed token and a numeric key buys no type change (TOML keys are always strings, and `001` and `1` are two legal DIFFERENT keys, which is a near-miss the prefixed form does not have). cell_to_value types by COLUMN, not by guessing: REF_COLS become arrays, INT_COLS become ints, an empty cell becomes an absent key, and everything else stays a string - this is a carrier change, not a schema change. toml_scalar emits multi-line basic strings for anything long or newline-bearing (tomllib is read-only by design, so the kit emits its own, as it already does in wi_convert.toml_string and bootstrap.set_process_key). sn_to_toml keeps the edge-case tier's own lifecycle/scenario/expected fields as THEMSELVES and writes kind= from the heading, retiring section-as-state. compare() re-reads what was emitted and diffs it against an {id: {key: text}} projection built from either carrier, so ONE comparison serves both instead of a second copy that drifts.
Rationale
The round-trip check is the requirement, not a convenience - and a check that cannot fail would be the false green SN-008 forbids, which is why the loss detector is driven against real corruption classes rather than only against a clean run. Ref cells compare on the TOKEN LIST because the conversion re-joins them with the canonical separator and the original spacing was never part of the meaning; comparing raw text there would report a loss that is not one. The edge-case fold that traj_parse._sn_fields performs is a PRESENTATION rule the markdown table forced, so it stays in the exporters - baking it into the carrier would make the exporter's reading the only reading there is.

LLR-ID
LLR-166
SR-Refs
SR-147
Title
The spine carrier's one reader, and the dual-home refusal
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spine_carrier.py
CodeSymbol
SPINE_TABLE/SPINE_COLUMN/rows_from_toml/rows_from_text/resolve/load
Detail
PURE like trace_text (text in, rows out; no git, no argv) except resolve()'s existence check, because the dual-home refusal is the one rule that must not be duplicated. rows_from_toml presents rows under TODAY'S COLUMN NAMES via SPINE_COLUMN - the exact inverse of migrate_carrier.KEY - so the carrier change is data plus a loader rather than a sixteen-module rename; SPINE_TABLE is keyed on the ID COLUMN, not on a path, because a path carries a carrier suffix and therefore moves. An absent key stays absent rather than becoming "", and a decode error returns None rather than {}. resolve() RAISES when both carriers exist rather than picking by precedence, and load() raises on a carrier that exists and will not parse. carriers() returns BOTH paths so an applicability test spans the cutover.
Rationale
None-vs-{} and absent-vs-empty are the same defect twice: {} says the registry had no rows, which for a baseline read means re-bless everything with no diff and for a gate means nothing to check, so a carrier that cannot be read must be ABSENT rather than empty. The dual-home REFUSAL follows the rule bootstrap --migrate-config already applies to the policy dials: a precedence rule lets a half-finished migration keep working while quietly reading the stale half, so the conversion is never finished and nobody finds out. ONE HOME for the vocabulary is an owner ruling (2026-08-10, repo-lock D-6) amending F5: duplicating a vocabulary fails silently - the copy that has not learned a column returns a row missing that cell, which every consumer reads as "the cell is empty".

SR-148Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what orderre-attest owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-148current content
SR-ID
SR-148
Title
Autonomous loop work selection: what it derives from, and in what order
SN-Refs
SN-025;SN-029;SN-027;SN-006;SN-008;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall select the work an unattended run does next from the repository's tracked registries and git history alone — shipping no hand-curated next-work or run-phase pointer surface for any live instruction or executable surface to read, write, validate, generate or link, and deriving from no prose surface and no predefined track — in this order: ready adjudication rows first, as a stable partition applied at admission rather than by renumbering the ruled rank table; then unresolved handback records; then the earliest incomplete spine tier in SN-to-SR-to-LLR-to-TC order; then implementation work after test-case layout is complete — with the eligible set and its order deterministic, an item whose declared safety, policy or plan-mode inputs are missing, undeclared or contradictory failing closed for that item alone, human holds applied only from the declared ratification level, nothing admitted past a human-held stop, and the status surface a session reads generated and freshness-gated rather than hand-copied.
Rationale
Without one precedence rule, the same repository can resume into implementation while returned obligations or prerequisite requirement tiers remain unresolved. That produces work against stale intent and makes repeated resumes select different next actions. A recorded selection class, source record and hold decision provide replayable evidence of the choice. Absorbs SR-141 (census F5, ruled overlap): both rows stated where adjudication ranks at admission and the acceptance texts had already diverged in nuance — one SR per (SN-025, loop selection order) per 13p; SR-141's stable-partition mechanism lands as an LLR under this row, and its id retires under the D-1/D-4 doctrine with the forwarding record in the log. ABSORBS TWO MORE, 2026-08-15, on the same doctrine and the same invariant: the top-down read found SN-025's loop work-selection stated THREE times — this row, SR-153 ("Ready work derives from tracked state") and SR-059 ("Retire next-wi; generated status surface") — with the ordering claim made twice and "no hand-maintained pointer" three times, against 13p's one-SR-per-(need, property) rule. SR-153 contributed the derivation source (tracked registries and git history alone), the determinism of the eligible set and its order, and the per-item fail-closed rule for missing, undeclared or contradictory declared inputs; SR-059 contributed the no-pointer-surface obligation in its strong form (no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links one) and the generated, freshness-gated status surface. Both ids retire; `sn_refs` unions to SN-025 + SN-029 + SN-027 + SN-006 + SN-008 + SN-010 so no need loses its carrier, and every shed clause is token-checked into this row's requirement or acceptance with the map recorded in the log. The alternative — an explicit three-way partition — was rejected for the reason census F5 gave when the same question was asked of SR-141: the partition already existed textually and still produced duplicated acceptance. RESTORED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F5): SR-059 required the deletion of the two pointer files in BOTH directions — "fresh scaffold AND migrated repo" — and the merge carried the fresh-scaffold half only, so an adopter upgrade could retain the retired authority files while this row still passed. The migrated-repo/resync half is back in the acceptance; it is the half that matters most, because a fresh scaffold never had the files to keep. Fan-out re-stamp: this row states one decision — what an unattended run's next work derives from and in what order — and its children are the successive stages of that one selection, not separable contracts; the width is the deliberate consolidation recorded above, which folded three rows stating the same invariant into one, so re-splitting to satisfy the bound would restore the duplication that merge was convened to remove.
AcceptanceCriteria
A ready adjudication row admits ahead of every other class, the ruled rank table unchanged (--explain still reports it) and non-adjudication rows keeping their relative order; an unresolved handback is selected before spine or implementation work, and processing it either resolves the record or mints a provenance-linked successor exactly once; with no unresolved handback, only the earliest incomplete spine tier is eligible; implementation work is ineligible while any required test case remains unlaid; at human_ratification_through = 0 no spine tier produces a human hold, levels 1 through 4 hold exactly their documented cumulative tiers, and a human-held attestation still drains and surfaces with the adjudication row waiting behind the stop; every selection records the chosen class, source identifier and hold decision. Two resumes over the same tracked state select the same eligible set in the same order; an item with missing or contradictory declared inputs is excluded with a stated reason while disjoint classified work stays eligible; no prose surface participates in the derivation; the ordering and exclusion reasons are exposed for replay. A fresh scaffold ships no next-wi/run-phase pointer and is parallel-by-default; no live instruction or executable surface reads, writes, validates, generates or links such a pointer; a build-out lane's phase resolves from its branch name with no pointer file; an upgraded or resynced repository that once carried such a pointer carries neither pointer file after the migration and no live dependency on either survives, so the deletion holds for a migrated repository and not only for a fresh scaffold; the status snapshot regenerates mechanically and its freshness is gated.

The adjudication kind: rank-1 slot + no-bar arm + the bar strictness key

Detail

Title
Admission order and the red-TC census
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/dispatch.py
CodeSymbol
_judgement_first/_admission/red_tc_census/parse_red_tc/_implemented_ids
Detail
`_judgement_first` is a STABLE PARTITION over the frontier, applied inside `_admission` before the surface/dispatch split so it can never move work past a human-held stop. `red_tc_census` rides the existing finding-string seam with a distinct prefix; `_red_tc_line` and `parse_red_tc` sit adjacent because the grammar is a contract between two modules, and `intake` routes on the parser rather than re-splitting prose. red_tc_census names, as its own class, a test case that is not green while every requirement it verifies is cited by a work item recorded done — Approved, Drafted and Modified test cases are exempt, which under the narrowed enum is the whole closed vocabulary, so the census is structurally dead in a conformant repo (retire-or-re-arm is the owner's recorded judgement item, 2026-08-15 sitting sweep); an open, partial or cancelled citer makes nothing red, and a test case verifying a mix of claimed and unclaimed targets is not red; each named line routes to an adjudication row whose suggested tier is estimated from measured breadth, never defaulted; the census line's grammar has exactly one reader (parse_red_tc).
Rationale
The alternative to the partition — renumbering `schedule._KIND_RANK` — would change ordering for every downstream repo for a rung that belongs to admission. The alternative to the parser is prose carrying control flow, which is the exact defect the typed `suggested_tier` field replaced.

SR-146;SR-144;SR-148
Title
The adjudicator briefs' evidence, and the refusal that keeps a half-filled one unsent
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/adjudicate_brief.py
CodeSymbol
compose/disposition_values/red_tc_values
Detail
The row's DECLARED `Brief` cell selects the template (`intake` writes it at every adjudication mint); `compose` fills it through `prompts.fill`'s strict both-ways check and `agent_loop.session_body` is the single fork BOTH routing arms take, so a claimed adjudication row gets the same brief whether or not a routing registry is configured. Each assembler returns `(values, None)` or `(None, reason)` and NEVER a partial: `disposition_values` walks SpecRef -> the closed spec -> its immutable per-close report -> that report's TYPED `commit_range` -> `git log`/`--name-status` clipped at the declared 80 lines, and refuses the clean-close spot-check arm because it writes no report; `red_tc_values` RE-RUNS `dispatch.red_tc_census` rather than remembering it, joins each line through `dispatch.parse_red_tc` to the TC row's Method/Expected/Evidence and to the SR/LLR text its targets name, and refuses a census that has come clean. A refusal falls back to the worker assignment and PRINTS why. `conflict` and `amendment` are deliberately unrouted, each with its missing derivation named in the module header.
Rationale
Two rules decide the shape. A judge's brief must not carry the claim under judgement as its premise - a leaked self-assessment anchors the judgement to it - so every value is registry-, git- or report-derived and the one claim shown - the lane's report - is the template's own labelled subject. And a HALF-filled brief is worse than the generic prompt: hollow sections read as an investigation that was run and found nothing, which is the expensive way to be wrong, so there is no placeholder path at all. The discriminator is a declared cell rather than a SpecRef shape because that inference is unsound - an amendment to a test-case row and a red-TC census row both cite the TC registry while their briefs give contradictory instructions - and rather than a Title substring because that is the `NEEDS-HUMAN` fold (LLR-161).

SR-149Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfacesre-attest owed

SR SR-149current content
SR-ID
SR-149
Title
Retired-vocabulary refusal in authored surfaces
SN-Refs
SN-004;SN-010
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The harness shall report every occurrence of a retired process tag in a live authored surface — naming the file and line and the vocabulary that replaces it, at a severity that warns by default and fails under --strict — reporting none in a declared historical, generated or attestation-quoting surface, nor in a line or file marked as a declaration site.
Rationale
Realizes SN-004 (the ladder's vocabulary is the one the project is held to) and SN-010 (docs stay honest). OI-21 made this check a CONDITION of the vocabulary conversion rather than a follow-up, on measured evidence: an earlier sweep removed a retired-tag construct from this repo's own open-items registry and it REGENERATED within days, caught only by an audit. A ~2,500-edit conversion held in place by attention is a conversion that comes undone. The carve-outs are as load-bearing as the rule: history is not rewritten and an attestation is never re-worded, because a swept sign-off makes a signed record claim something was signed that was not. Hat-derived (hat.MAINTAINER): C-MNT-3 gives every declared vocabulary value exactly one normative definition in one home; a retired tag surviving in a live authored surface is a second, contradictory definition of the same word, which is why the earlier sweep regenerated within days when only attention held it. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-16 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
check_vocab.py reports a planted retired tag in a live authored file with its path and line, exits 0 under the default severity and 1 under --strict; reports nothing for a file under any declared carve-out class (archive, the project log, attestation briefs, closed and cancelled work-item specs, generated surfaces) including at arbitrary depth; reports nothing for a line carrying the allow marker or a file carrying the allow-file marker; and reports nothing for the word "gate" used to mean a check that can fail.

SR-149
Title
Retired-vocabulary enforcer
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_vocab.py
CodeSymbol
scan
Detail
Walks the tree, classifies each file through in_scope (declared carve-out globs for historical, generated and attestation-quoting surfaces, plus a text-suffix filter), and reports one finding per offending LINE - not per tag, since a line naming three tags is one sentence to rewrite. Matching is TAG-SCOPED (a whole-word alternation over the retired G tags), never the word 'gate', which survives wherever it means a check that can fail. Declaration sites opt out through an explicit in-file marker rather than a heuristic, because a heuristic that can be satisfied by accident is how the vocabulary grows back through the enforcer meant to stop it. Wired into check.py at EVERY bar with --strict added from DevBar-Tests on, mirroring check_trajectory's severity ladder: the surfaces the tags regrow into are authored hardest at the lowest bar.

SR-152Hosted CI verdict is the harness's ownapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-152current content
SR-ID
SR-152
Title
Hosted CI verdict is the harness's own
SN-Refs
SN-008;SN-005
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The hosted CI job verdict shall carry the harness's own exit for the declared bar — the job failing when the harness exits nonzero, with a step log naming each step's outcome and no silently skipped required step — as the declared backstop re-run behind the bypassable local hook floor.
Rationale
Realizes SN-008 (a reader can believe a green) and SN-005. This is the second half of the honest-limit pair SR-019 states from the local side: a local floor is bypassable (git commit --no-verify), so no-unchecked-write-enters-governed-state rests on the verdict at the moment of the act PLUS a re-run of the same bar away from that bypass. Attributed to B-05, the delivered package, on the DESIGN-CONTROL reading (owner ruling 2026-08-16, applied to SR-151 first and to this row on the same argument): the shall constrains what the SHIPPED WORKFLOW declares — no continue-on-error, no conditional skip, the harness's own exit as the job's — and the system holds no authority over an external runner honouring it. STATED SO IT IS NOT READ AS COVERAGE: the re-run therefore has no crossing of its own in this frame. That is the ruling's deliberate consequence, not an oversight — a hosted runner is an ADOPTER's boundary, and the frame already handles an uncontrollable external the same way at REL-003, where the provider-surface obligation lands on delivered loop content rather than minting a crossing for EXT-005. SR-114 keeps the portability obligation; the CI matrix as an evidence surface is this row's.
AcceptanceCriteria
The shipped workflow contains no continue-on-error or conditional skip around a required harness step; a nonzero harness exit fails the job; the step log names the harness invocation and its tier. This row is the backstop half of the pair SR-019 states from the local side.
Priority
M
Verification
Test
Status
Drafted

SR-154Independent review routed across familiesapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-154current content
SR-ID
SR-154
Title
Independent review routed across families
SN-Refs
SN-026;SN-024;SN-006
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
Before unattended work integrates, the delivered loop content shall obtain each review or critique verdict the declared policy requires from a session that did not author the work — resolved per in-process phase and tier from the delivered agent registry's declared (family x model x tier) rows and only while the declared consent surface that turns managed selection on is present, drawn from a different model family wherever one is configured, degrading only to the documented same-family mode — with every selection logged before launch and a non-converging rework loop escalated through the declared ratification level.
Rationale
Realizes SN-026 (cross-family second opinions — every prior carrier of this contract demoted as routing machinery, so the sn_refs join forced the mint; SR-155 cites SN-026 too, for the planner pair, but only this row states the second opinion itself), SN-024 (an author cannot judge its own output) and SN-006. One row because routing, scheduling, scoring and escalation are one delivered contract — that the second opinion happens, independently, or its absence is documented — whose mechanics are the component details below it. Extended 2026-08-15: SN-026's DECLARATION and CONSENT half — `docs/agents.toml`'s pair-rows and the `docs/agents-enabled` surface whose presence turns managed selection on — had ZERO textual occurrence anywhere in the SR layer while `orphans=0` still held, because an orphan count proves a need has a citing row and never that the row carries the whole need. Managed selection is opt-in by consent rather than by configuration alone: a repository that declares families without dropping the surface has declared a capability, not turned one on, and the row now says so where a check can read it.
AcceptanceCriteria
A declared review policy of N schedules N independent reviewer sessions; the declared (family x model x tier) pair-rows in docs/agents.toml are the only candidate source and docs/agents-enabled is the consent surface — absent, managed selection is off and no row is resolved; present, exactly one row resolves per in-process phase and tier; a configured cross-family draw is preferred and its unavailability degrades to the documented mode, never a silent skip; every selection is logged before launch, never a silent model swap; rework iterates within the declared budget and pages per the declared ratification level on exhaustion.

SR-155Contested planning rounds for marked workapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-155current content
SR-ID
SR-155
Title
Contested planning rounds for marked work
SN-Refs
SN-024;SN-006;SN-026;SN-002
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
For work declared as contested planning, the delivered loop content shall produce the selected decomposition through a bounded round of two independently authored rival plans — cross-critiqued, mechanically coverage-compared and arbitrated by fresh sessions — committing the selected outcome atomically as registry-valid queued rows and surfacing a non-converging round through the declared ratification level, never falling back to a single uncontested plan silently.
Rationale
Realizes SN-024 (a decomposition judged by a competing plan and an independent arbiter rather than its author), with SN-026 (the planner pair prefers distinct families), SN-002 (the selected outcome lands as registry-valid rows, not prose) and SN-006. One row because the dual-plan round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class (SELECT or PAGE); its state machine, briefs, adapters and isolation rules are the component tier's. Fan-out re-stamp: the round is one delivered capability with one observable outcome class, and its children are the successive phases of that single round — planner pair, briefs, coverage comparison, arbitration, isolation, disposition — none of which an adopter can obtain, refuse, or observe independently of the round, so the child count measures the round's step count rather than a merge of contracts.
AcceptanceCriteria
A row marked for contested planning is refused on the direct build path; the round runs planner pair, cross-critique, mechanical coverage comparison and position-swapped arbitration as fresh sessions within declared budgets; SELECT files children that pass the registry validator atomically; PAGE preserves the round's evidence and maps through the declared ratification level.
Priority
S
Verification
Test
Status
Drafted
Phase

LLR-ID
LLR-132
SR-Refs
SR-155
Title
PAGE ratification-level mapping
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
main
Detail
Applies the declared level's continuation semantics to a PAGE at both the dispatcher disposition and the --dual-plan single-shot round: `page_action(human_held, keep_nondependent)` over the four cells the retired enum's three words could not span — a human-held tier pages, a loop-held one STALLs, and `None` normalizes to the strictest cell rather than to `bool(None)`'s most permissive one. A PAGE preserves the round's evidence; the human-held cell exits NEEDS-HUMAN and a loop-held one exits STALL with no human gate.
Rationale
Split from the disposition row: committing the outcome and deciding whether it stops a human are independent, and the second is shared with a second entry point that would otherwise drift.

SR SR-156current content
SR-ID
SR-156
Title
Bounded lanes narrowing to one gated landing
SN-Refs
SN-027;SN-008;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall run ready work on bounded, mutually isolated lanes and narrow every lane back to the integration branch through one serial, fail-closed seam that runs the declared bar on the composed tree — parking a red candidate and stopping the queue loudly, draining claiming to a clean merged stop under a declared pause, and reconstructing claim and queue state from version-control history alone at any crash boundary, never a half-integrated authoritative state.
Rationale
Realizes SN-027 WHOLE — ready work fans out across bounded parallel lanes and that fan-out narrows to one serialized gated seam before anything lands, in the need's own words — with SN-008 (the seam is fail-closed) and SN-025 (recovery from version-control history alone). SPLIT 2026-08-16 (re-tier v2 S4, ruling R1): the row's former "one row because serial integration is one delivered contract" argument was sound for the SEAM, but three children were not the seam — lane execution, claim authority, and the trunk-side mint of work-item identity had ridden in under it. The measured corroboration: no SR in the corpus stated SN-027's FIRST half at all (a grep of every requirement cell for bounded lanes, isolated working trees or a worker ceiling returned nothing), so the merge was hiding a coverage hole rather than expressing one contract — exactly what the fan-out detector exists to surface. This row therefore widens to the honest lane LIFECYCLE (claim, run isolated, close, land) and closes that hole in the same act; the trunk-only authority contract sheds to SR-170. The two fail independently: a perfectly serialized landing that lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies this row and not that one. OVERREACH RETRACTED 2026-08-16 (S6 second top-down read, finding M4): the widening had also taken "closing a lane that cannot finish into a terminal state", which is SR-144's shall verbatim in substance ("close a lane that cannot finish into a TERMINAL partial state that merges like any other branch") under the same SN-027 — two rows for one observable, against 13p, and the sign of it was LLR-144 sitting here while SR-144 stated its contract. The clause and its handback.py carrier are struck; what remains is this row's own half, that a terminal branch still lands through the one serial seam. LLR-144 re-parents to SR-144 with TC-138.
AcceptanceCriteria
A launch with independent ready work runs up to the configured lane ceiling concurrently in isolated working trees, and a single-lane setting preserves the serial semantic; a finished branch reaches the integration branch only through the serial seam with the declared bar green on the composed tree — a red parks the candidate and stops the queue loudly; a declared pause stops claiming and drains in-flight lanes to a merged stop; a lane closed under the terminal-close contract (SR-144's, not restated here) reaches the integration branch through this same serial seam and by no other path; a crash at any lifecycle boundary recovers from version-control history without double-assignment. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: integrate.py, lane.py, agent_common.py's tracked pause reader.

Title
Link-aware spec-move ritual (move + relink indivisible)
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/spec_move.py
CodeSymbol
move_spec/expected_relink/archive_dest
Detail
move_spec moves a registry/spec file and keeps every markdown link in the repo true as ONE indivisible operation: the moved file's own relative targets are re-relativised against its new directory, every inbound link whose target resolves to the vacated path is redirected (link text untouched, #fragments carried, external/protocol-relative/bare-anchor targets left alone, root-relative targets excused only in the rebase half, line endings preserved via newline=''), and every rewritten file is staged with the move so the ritual lands as one changeset. integrate.claim and handback.close_partial call it for the claim and close moves; the CLI performs the worker's terminal close move and the dated docs/archive/specs/ archival. expected_relink is the pure oracle integrate._abandoned_claim compares byte-for-byte to excuse a crashed claim's own relink writes without widening its conviction to any .md edit.
Rationale
A bare move strands both link directions and the break surfaces late, on the composed tree (measured: one probe archival took the broken-link count from 4 to 8 - 3 outbound, 1 inbound). Making move+relink one indivisible operation is a constraint that leaves the two-thirds state unrepresentable, rather than a check that detects it after the parallel work is done.

Title
Spine and work-registry rules red the harness verdict
SN-Refs
SN-002;SN-012;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report every declared spine and work-registry rule violation — broken joins, integrity and schema faults, leftover placeholders, requirement-form findings and work-item coherence contradictions — naming the at-fault row and cell, gating the declared failure set at the declared gate while advisory classes never change the exit code.
Rationale
Realizes SN-002 (the chain is mechanically verified, not asserted — this row is the boundary-level home of the verdict SN-002's acceptance asks for), SN-012 (optional registries and opt-outs cost a non-user nothing) and SN-025 (queue-overlap visibility). One row because the adopter-observable contract is one: registry rules produce named findings at a declared severity; which rules, at which severities, is the component detail the rows below it decompose. Deliberate pair (census F6): the PB-format invariant itself is SR-015's; this row's decomposition carries the checker. Fan-out re-stamp: the delivered contract is one — a named, row-and-cell-attributed finding at a declared severity, gating or advisory — while the rule inventory it ranges over is open by this row's own acceptance ("a rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default"), so the child count tracks the number of declared rules rather than the number of decisions this row makes, and would climb back through any bound a split bought.
AcceptanceCriteria
Each declared registry rule violation is reported with row-level attribution; the declared strict set exits nonzero at its gate and advisory classes never join the exit code; absent or placeholder-only optional registries and declared opt-outs are vacuous; the declared verification vocabulary (including Critique) is accepted and an unknown value fails. The declaration this is read against is, as the current set: trace.py's `ID_PATTERNS`, `REQUIRED_FIELDS` and `ENUM_FIELDS` (the id shapes, the required cells, and the closed vocabularies -- SR `Verification`, TC `Tier` -- with the IF/CMP entries advisory by their own recorded ruling), the `--strict` / `--strict-integrity` / `--strict-schema` / `--no-placeholders` flag families and the exit contract stated in that module's docstring; trace_text.py's `form_findings` and `provenance_findings` (gating) beside `paraphrase_advisories` and `ac_advisories` (advisory, never the exit code); and check_trajectory.py's work-item rules R-A, R-D, R-E and R-F with `docs/process.toml [checks] trajectory_check` as the declared opt-out. A rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default: an unreported one is a finding against this row, not an amendment to it.

Title
Spine stand-alone rule
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
provenance_findings
Detail
provenance_findings flags a WI id or a process-doc citation across the normative cells of all three spine registries, joining the --strict failure set via exit_code. The normative cells are the SR Title/Requirement/Rationale/AcceptanceCriteria, the LLR Title/Detail/Rationale, and the TC Method/Expected/Parameters; pointer columns are out of scope, and each finding names the registry, row and cell. Not a finding: an artifact path, a rubric, a sibling spine id, and a port-script name — the ten declared external ports (check.py, bootstrap.py, agent_loop.py, check_vendored.py, gen_cases.py, gen_release_checklist.py, subagent_gate.py, run_menu.py, integrate.py, trunk_step.py) — the script-name carve-out reaching no wider than that set.
Rationale
A citation is a pointer a later reader may be unable to follow, and the row's reason travels with it: when the work item is closed and archived, the row keeps its claim and loses its justification, which is the failure this catches. Applied to every spine registry rather than the SR alone, because the layer the rule could not originally see held the largest pocket of violations — a narrower scope would guard the cleanest layer and miss the dirtiest.

LLR LLR-134current content
LLR-ID
LLR-134
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Requirement form rules
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
form_findings
Detail
form_findings applies six rules — one obligation per requirement, no non-obligation modal, no actorless passive, no unfalsifiable term, no open-ended clause, no obligation keyword in an LLR Detail — skipping Draft rows, and joins the --strict failure set via exit_code. The negative set holds: a Draft row, a zero-obligation row, a project's own declared alternate obligation keyword, and a multi-clause AcceptanceCriteria are not findings — stated keyword-free here deliberately, since the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use.
Rationale
A row carrying two obligations reports neither when one fails, and undecidable wording is signed on impression rather than evidence — that is the failure all six rules exist to prevent. Draft is skipped because a Draft row is exempt from the child-completeness rules elsewhere in this harness and is by convention the state a requirement occupies while still being written, so an unfinished acceptance criterion is expected there rather than defective; flagging it would make the state unusable for the drafting it exists to allow. This cell says "obligation keyword" rather than quoting the keyword itself because the rule cannot distinguish a mention from a use, and an exemption a checker cannot see is one an author can reach for.
TestRefs
(see TC)

Modified
Component
CMP-006
Phase

LLR-ID
LLR-135
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
Paraphrase advisory
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace_text.py
CodeSymbol
paraphrase_advisories
Detail
paraphrase_advisories compares a child cell's significant words (alphabetic runs of four characters or more, lowercased) against its parent's and reports when the share of the CHILD's words that also appear in the parent exceeds the declared threshold — the child is the denominator, so a long parent cannot dilute a short echoing child. Reported in its own advisory pipe, which never joins a failure set. The two watched relationships are an SR Rationale against its own Requirement and an LLR Detail against its parent SR Requirement — the pairs where the parent is unambiguous; each finding emits a stdout WARNING plus a report line with the exit code unchanged.
Rationale
A child that re-words its parent adds a second home for one fact and no decomposition, so a later amendment updates one copy and the two disagree with nothing to report it. The advisory has its own pipe rather than sharing the acceptance-criteria counter because a shared count would report 'ac-advisories' about a finding that is not one, and a reader acts on the counter name.
TestRefs
(see TC)

LLR LLR-160current content
LLR-ID
LLR-160
SR-Refs
SR-157
Title
The mechanical queue-overlap pre-filter
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_trajectory.py
CodeSymbol
queue_conflict_findings/_title_tokens
Detail
Three signals over OPEN rows only — Jaccard over title token sets at a deliberately high bar, shared `SR-Refs`, and a shared anchor-stripped `SpecRef` — each reported once per pair per signal with both ids, in sorted order. Printed on the warn-only line beside backlog staleness; never appended to `errors`. A shared stopword alone is never a signal, and the findings stay out of the exit code even under --strict.
Rationale
A low similarity bar turns a useful warn into noise that gets ignored, which is worse than not having the warn: this rung catches the same job minted twice, not two rows in the same area.

SR-158Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tierapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-158current content
SR-ID
SR-158
Title
Documentation drift reds or warns per its declared tier
SN-Refs
SN-010;SN-008
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report a documentation surface that has drifted from what it claims — a broken intra-repo link, a missing vision declaration, a reference to an absent path or symbol, a stale generated doc, or a declared figure without its provenance — at the declared warn-or-gate severity for each class.
Rationale
Realizes SN-010 (navigable, honest documentation) and SN-008 (a green never hides a stale claim). One row because the delivered contract is one honesty verdict over the documentation surfaces; the individual checkers and their carve-outs are component details.
AcceptanceCriteria
A dead intra-repo link, an absent PROJECT-VISION tag, a stale generated doc under --stale, a dangling backticked path or sym: reference, and a declared fig: marker missing cmd/rev each produce a finding at their declared tier; the declared carve-outs (generated blocks, path-ok and fig-ok lines, unmarked figures, URLs and placeholders) stay silent. The tier for each class is read off its declaration site, as the current set: check.py's built-in `doc-navigability` step, which runs check_docs.py from DevBar-Reqs on -- a broken intra-repo link and a missing or repeated PROJECT-VISION tag exit nonzero, orphans warn unless --strict-orphans, and --stale is warn-only; docs/stack.ini's `[step:doc-refs]` and `[step:figures]` rows, which declare `gates = DevBar-Release` for check_doc_refs.py --strict (gating on dangling alone, with untraced counted and never gating) and check_figures.py --strict; and the carve-out surfaces those checkers read -- `docs/orphans-allow`, `docs/declared-absences`, and the path-ok / fig-ok line markers. A class whose severity is stated at none of those sites is undeclared, and this row is unsatisfied until it is declared there.

Title
Path + sym: reference checker
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_doc_refs.py
CodeSymbol
findings_for/load_symbol_oracle/is_path_shaped
Detail
Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md; a backticked token is path-shaped when it carries a separator plus a known extension or top-level prefix (URLs/globs/placeholders excluded); GENERATED marker blocks and path-ok lines are skipped; sym:<module>.<name> is resolved against the module map parsed from architecture.md (module tail -> public symbols); warn-first, --strict exits 1. With no module-map inventory the symbol tier skips cleanly while the path tier still runs.

SR-158
Title
Declared-figure provenance presence check
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check_figures.py
CodeSymbol
findings_for/judge_marker/marker_segments
Detail
Scans root *.md + docs/**/*.md (check_doc_refs.doc_files - the same walk, so the two doc checks agree on the surface and generated trees stay skipped) plus docs/stack.ini, with docs/reviews/ records out of scope (a verdict quotes defective markers as evidence); each opt-in fig: marker is judged on its own attributes and must carry a non-empty, wordful cmd= and rev= pair (rev bare or quoted), or a non-empty derived= derivation; a marker is grammar prose that declares nothing only when every attribute value is wholly a placeholder token (angle-bracket-wrapped, or the bare ellipsis - so the convention text ships scaffold-clean); in a judged marker a placeholder-shaped value counts as absent, never as satisfying (an unfilled rev=<revision> names no revision), while a metacharacter inside a longer value is command text and the marker is judged on its attributes; findings name the missing half and quote the line; GENERATED marker blocks and fig-ok lines are exempt; warn-first, --strict exits 1. Presence only, never truth - re-derivation is rung 2, deliberately not built.
Rationale
Would have flagged all three 2026-08-01 false figures; opt-in keeps the claim honest - declared figures carry provenance, not all figures do.

SR-159Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reportedapproval owed

SR SR-159current content
SR-ID
SR-159
Title
Declared-architecture connectivity gaps are reported
SN-Refs
SN-023;SN-002;SN-012
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered harness shall report declared-architecture connectivity gaps — an unresolvable interface row, an undeclared endpoint or direction, a cross-component import no declared interface covers, an invalid spec interface citation, and a top view past its declared bound — warn-first with --strict gating, vacuous where the optional inventories are absent.
Rationale
Realizes SN-023 (the parts' connections are as much of the truth as the requirement tree, checked mechanically warn-first — the need's own words), SN-002 (interface rows stay resolvable) and SN-012 (opt-out and vacuity keep the layer free for non-users). One row: the delivered contract is one connectivity verdict; the checkers below it are its decomposition.
AcceptanceCriteria
Malformed or unresolvable IF rows are findings; undeclared endpoints, uncovered cross-component edges, invalid spec citations and an oversized top view warn plain and fail --strict; declared opt-outs and small or absent inventories are vacuous; declared seams render without changing the verdict. The rules and their opt-outs are read off their declaration sites, as the current set: trace.py for IF/CMP id shape and the off-spine back-link to the spine (`ID_PATTERNS` and the always-on integrity floor, which joins --strict), its `REQUIRED_FIELDS["IF"]` / `["CMP"]` entries and their enums staying advisory by the recorded warn-first sequencing; check_trajectory.py for the connectivity rules -- every arch-map module resolving to at least one IF endpoint (the "connectivity undeclared" warn), a cross-component import no IF row covers, a spec's `## Interfaces` section citing an unresolvable IF-### or neither a seam nor the intra-module escape, and the `TOP_VIEW_MAX` bound of 10 items (warn plain, error under --strict); `docs/process.toml [checks] interfaces_check` and `components_check` as the two declared opt-outs, with a `source`/`sink` token in an IF row's Notes as the per-module honesty valve; and gen_arch_map.py's generated block in docs/architecture.md as the module inventory every one of those rules joins against. A connectivity rule added at one of those sites is in this row's scope by default. Carved out of that default (owner ruling 2026-08-15, second-read M1): the interface endpoint-pair cells and the closed discrete/variable Signal vocabulary are SR-162's observables — their resolution against the declared external frame is SN-037's need, stated there in the need's own words — while the generic required-field and closed-vocabulary checker those cells ride stays SR-157's; a rule landing at those two cells belongs to those rows, not this one.

SR-165The component partition carries a reproducible derivation recordapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-165current content
SR-ID
SR-165
Title
The component partition carries a reproducible derivation record
SN-Refs
SN-040
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered planning content shall leave, with every component partition it produces, a machine-readable derivation record in the tracked component registry rather than session prose — the candidate partitions considered, the declared objective and constraints they were scored against, each candidate's score, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it — such that a live component row whose record omits any of those elements, or a selection the recorded scores do not rank first without a recorded human override, is a reportable finding.
Rationale
Realizes SN-040 — a free-form partition leaves no record of the alternatives weighed, so the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents; the record is what lets the choice be re-examined. SN-040 had no citing SR, even as the P5 partition work performed exactly this record. Reproducibility is stated as a recomputation from the record's own declared inputs, not as a reviewer re-running a model session, because only the former can fail — the SR-161 form applied to the partition instead of the perspective set. B-05 alone: the human ruling is an element the record must carry, not an observable the system states at the authority input, which is SR-139's and SR-140's ground.
AcceptanceCriteria
A partition round writes the derivation record into the tracked component registry, never into session prose or a plan document alone; a live component row with no record, or a record omitting the candidate partitions, the declared objective, the constraints, any candidate's score, the selected partition or the human ruling, is reported naming the missing element; a selected partition the recorded scores do not rank first is reported unless the record carries the human override that chose it; the recorded ranking recomputes from the record's own objective, constraints and scores alone.

LLR LLR-172current content
LLR-ID
LLR-172
SR-Refs
SR-165
Title
Component derivation record: element completeness + the ranking recompute
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/trace.py
CodeSymbol
component_findings
Detail
Extends the existing component pass — the one home that already reads docs/requirements/components.toml and already emits CMP findings — with the derivation-record rules SR-165 states, rather than adding a second reader of the same registry. Per live CMP row: the record must declare the candidate partitions considered, the objective and the constraints they were scored against, a score per candidate, the selected partition, and the human ruling that named the components and adopted it; a missing element is one finding naming the row AND the element. Two derived rules on top of presence, because presence alone is satisfiable by a record that says nothing: (1) THE RANKING RECOMPUTES — re-rank the recorded candidates from the record's own objective, constraints and scores and compare with the recorded order, a mismatch being a finding, which is what makes the record falsifiable without a reviewer re-running a model session; (2) A SELECTION THE SCORES DO NOT RANK FIRST is a finding unless the record carries a human override naming that choice, so a legitimate "the scores said A, the human chose B" is expressible and an unexplained one is not. Vacuous by construction on a project declaring no components, and on the example -000 rows. NOT BUILT YET: the record cells do not exist on the CMP schema today, which is why this row and TC-166 are Draft — SR-165 was flipped Inspection -> Test at act 7 precisely because the check is mechanizable, and this is the debt that flip incurred, recorded rather than silently owed.
Rationale
The check belongs where component_findings already is for the reason the census keeps applying: a second module reading components.toml would be a second answer to "is this partition recorded", and the two would drift. The recompute is the load-bearing half — an element-presence check passes on a record whose scores contradict its own conclusion, which is the exact failure SN-040 exists to catch (the next reviser cannot tell load-bearing boundaries from accidents). The override arm is what stops the recompute rule from forbidding human judgement: the requirement is that a departure from the scores is RECORDED, never that it is disallowed.
TestRefs

Status
Drafted
Component

Phase

SR-166The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it willapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-166current content
SR-ID
SR-166
Title
The delivered package materializes where its manifest declares it will
SN-Refs
SN-001;SN-007
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered package shall be consumable at its own declared shipped-file manifest — every file the manifest names materialized at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and this repository's own instance carrying the structure of the template it ships — with a declared destination a fresh scaffold does not carry, and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's instance, each failing rather than degrading the delivered package silently.
Rationale
Realizes SN-001 (an adopting team gets a working, gated, requirement-traced process without hand-building the tooling — which presupposes that what arrives is whole) and SN-007 (the kit is held to the standard it ships). §1R.6 names this as B-05's own artifact-shaped observable — the package "exists, is complete, and is consumable" — and records that its manifest is already pinned; the 2026-08 re-tier campaign minted fifteen rows and not this one, leaving the crossing the frame describes at greatest length with no requirement stating it, and MAPPING surviving only in SR-163's rationale. Verification evidence is named rather than owed: tests/test_bootstrap.py drives bootstrap.py's MAPPING against the file lists a fresh scaffold must carry, and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py pins this repository's own instance against the template it ships, so the manifest cannot declare one thing and the package deliver another. Deliberately distinct from its two neighbours: SR-163 asks why each shipped file EXISTS (SN-038, purpose coverage), SR-010 asks whether the generated scaffold runs GREEN, and this row asks whether what was promised ARRIVES WHERE IT WAS PROMISED. NARROWED 2026-08-15 (adversarial round 2, cross-family, finding F7, convergent with the second read's H1): as minted, this row ALSO required that a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, each FAIL — and SR-163 already reports both classes under a configurable warn-versus-gate policy. Two rows prescribing different outcomes for one observable is a contradiction an implementer can only resolve by violating one of them, so the two manifest-PRESENCE clauses are shed here and referenced to SR-163; each was token-checked into SR-163's requirement ("missing files, stale inventory entries") and acceptance ("a shipped file absent from it", "a stale entry naming a file that no longer ships") before deletion, so no obligation vanished — only its second, conflicting statement. What is left is what SR-163 does not ask: not whether a file is accounted for, but whether it LANDS — at its declared destination in a fresh scaffold, and in this repository's own instance of the template it ships.
AcceptanceCriteria
A manifest entry whose destination a fresh scaffold does not contain fails naming the destination; and a structural divergence between the shipped template and this repository's own instance fails naming the surface. Evidence: tests/test_bootstrap.py (the manifest's destinations against the fresh-scaffold file lists) and tests/test_dogfood_sync.py (shipped template against this repository's instance). Manifest PRESENCE is deliberately NOT restated here — a named source the package does not carry, and a shipped file the manifest does not name, are both SR-163's, reported under its declared warn-versus-gate policy.

Extends LLR-051's process_panel with the project's two circular working loops as linked flow panels: (A) the intake loop - owner/agent intake -> triage into work items with spec detail -> the scheduler-derived ready frontier in the WI registry (docs/work/) -> build/review -> merge; (B) the human-decision loop - open-items population (incl. the gate-ratification table) -> human review and ruling -> the log's Decisions record -> merge - with the LLM_Agent entry node rendered once and shared by both loops. Each stage links to its canonical home (status.md, the WI registry (docs/work/), open-items.html, log.md); data-derived where a canonical source exists; a data-less repo renders byte-identically; --check unchanged (the trajectory-map step). Every stage link resolves to its canonical doc; generation is byte-deterministic; the loop panels are part of the adopted toolkit's generated project-state view (REL-002 output), not a system output.

SR-169The state view shows how the parts connectapproval owed

SR SR-169current content
SR-ID
SR-169
Title
The state view shows how the parts connect
SN-Refs
SN-023
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered generator set shall render the adopted repository's declared components and the interfaces between them as a navigable graph — showing which components sit inside which at more than one level, drawing an interface whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container to that container's edge, and showing a stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one when the repository carries the component and interface registries but declares no interface in them (a repository carrying no such registry at all omits the view under SR-070, not under this row).
Rationale
Realizes SN-023's SECOND conjunct, in the need's own words: the relationships between the parts (which module talks to which, over what contract) are as much of the project's truth as its requirement tree, and scattering them hides architectural drift. Minted 2026-08-16 by the re-tier v2 S4 split of SR-070 (ruling R1): the parent merged artifact integrity with view content, and the content half divides exactly where SN-023 divides. Kept separate from SR-168 rather than filed as one content row because the two fail independently and are built by different means — the progress views read the requirement and work registries, this one reads the declared component and interface registries, and either can be complete while the other is absent. The empty-view clause is load-bearing: a repository that has declared no interfaces yet must get a stated, legible surface, because an empty graph is indistinguishable from a broken generator. REWORDED 2026-08-16 for cold readability (adversarial round, finding F19, CONFIRMED): the row read "structural units", "seams terminating at the containing boundary" and "the artifact contract's omission rule" — vocabulary a first-time reader cannot resolve, the last of them an internal cross-reference with no id. Same obligations, plainer words, and the absent-registry case now names SR-070 explicitly; the empty-vs-absent split itself is unchanged. `phase` inherits the parent's 1; its children span 1 and 2, the ordinary child-later-than-parent case the 2026-08-14 ledger recorded as intended.
AcceptanceCriteria
Declared components render with their containment preserved at each visible level; each declared interface connects its correct endpoints, and one whose endpoint lies inside an unopened container is drawn once at that container's edge rather than duplicated; descending a level and returning restores the reader's prior place; a repository whose component and interface registries are present but declare no interface renders the stated "no interfaces declared" view rather than an empty one. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: the How-SW and interface-graph views of the root PROJECT_STATE.html as produced by gen_trajectory.py.

SR-170Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's aloneapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-170current content
SR-ID
SR-170
Title
Shared authority surfaces are the serial actor's alone
SN-Refs
SN-027;SN-010;SN-008;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall write the shared records it derives - the compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts - only from the serial merge step and only against the merged tree, never from a parallel work branch.
Rationale
SPLIT 2026-08-17 (Sol F18, applied): this row merged three obligations that fail independently, and the shared serial actor is a shared MECHANISM, not one decision. It keeps the EXCLUSIVE-WRITER contract; ordering-and-all-or-nothing left to SR-173 and identity allocation to SR-174, each with its own children. The clue Sol read correctly is that the non-reuse clause appeared only in this row's acceptance - an acceptance minting a requirement, the same defect as F12 - and it now has a shall of its own at SR-174. Terminology plained with the split: shared authority surface, mint, composed tree and serial integration seam are gone in favour of what they name. Realizes SN-027's serialization guarantee at the surfaces where a parallel writer would actually corrupt something, with SN-010 (the status surface a human reads regenerates at the seam, never on a lane), SN-008 (a first failure stops loudly rather than half-regenerating) and SN-025 (the surface a session reads is generated, never hand-copied). Minted 2026-08-16 by the re-tier v2 S4 split of SR-156 (ruling R1): the parent stated the serial LANDING and, separately, that the shared authority surfaces regenerate only there — two contracts that fail independently, since a perfectly serialized landing that still lets a lane commit a regenerated artifact satisfies the landing row and not this one. The three surfaces sit in ONE row rather than three because they share one decision, not three: the serial trunk actor is their sole writer. That decision is already owner-ruled for the identity mint (log.md Decisions 2026-08-01, R1/R3 — a work branch never mints a work-item id) and this row is its requirement-tier home, which it did not have. `phase` inherits the parent's 4, matching every child.
AcceptanceCriteria
The compiled activity log and the generated project-state artifacts are written only by the serial merge step and never committed from a work branch; a work branch that regenerates one is refused or its result is not what lands. Ordering and partial-result behaviour are SR-173's; identity allocation is SR-174's; the harness work-branch freshness skip is SR-006's clause and is not restated here. Read off the current carrier, as the current set: trunk_step.py (the log compile) and check.py's work-branch skip.

Title
Work-branch lane signal + freshness skip
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/check.py
CodeSymbol
_claimed_work_branch/steps
Detail
On a claimed work branch the _TRUNK_FRESHNESS_STEPS set SKIPs with a §5.2 notice, exit code untouched; the plan listing still names every step. The claim signal answers in two stages - docs/work/active/<branch>/ on disk (the fast path), else the branch's own committed history (git log -1 -- docs/work/active/<branch>) - so the closing commit that archives the claim, and every commit after it, keep the skip. No git, a failing git, or an empty answer all read as the trunk lane: a false work-branch positive would switch the freshness gates off on the one branch that owns regenerating them.
Rationale
A false work-branch positive would relax the trunk's freshness bar, so every ambiguity resolves toward the trunk lane.

SR-171Bounded retry on a declared transient model limitapproval owed

no baseline — no docs/archive/last_approved snapshot exists yet — this repo has approved nothing, so every row awaits a first approval

SR SR-171current content
SR-ID
SR-171
Title
Bounded retry on a declared transient model limit
SN-Refs
SN-006;SN-025
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall retry a declared transient provider limit under a bounded backoff rather than ending the run, and shall surface the wait so a reader can tell a throttled run from a wedged one.
Rationale
MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied). This obligation was live but stated ONLY in SR-026's acceptance cell, where it minted a requirement the shall never made. Deleting it would have dropped real behaviour, so it is stated here instead. Realizes SN-006 (a walk-away run survives the conditions it will actually meet - a rate limit is the ordinary one) and SN-025. The bound is what separates this from a retry loop: unbounded retry against a limit that is not transient is indistinguishable from a hang, which is the failure SN-006 exists to prevent. Surfacing the wait is the other half - a silent backoff and a wedged run look identical from outside.
AcceptanceCriteria
A declared transient limit is retried under the declared backoff and the run continues; the wait is visible on the run surface rather than silent; a limit that is not declared transient ends the run with its own outcome code rather than retrying; the retry count and interval come from a declared value, never a literal.

LLR-ID

SR-171
Title
Rate-limit recognition + bounded, surfaced wait
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
limit_reset_hint/seconds_until_reset/main
Detail
A rate limit is recognized only on an error signal (the JSON result's is_error, or a nonzero exit for a plain-text template) whose message carries a reset wording - a healthy transcript merely mentioning limits never reads as a throttle - and it wins the outcome ladder as WAITING, bypassing the stall guard. With --wait-on-limit set, a parsed reset within that ceiling is slept and the session retried, the sleep printed with its reset time; an unparseable wording sleeps min(--limit-retry-fallback, ceiling), printed, and retries. Past the ceiling, or without the flag, the run stops at EXIT_WAITING (5) with a banner naming the resume time. Both dials are declared flags with declared defaults (0 / 3600), never in-line literals. The outcome ladder itself (WAITING ahead of every other code) is LLR-028's home; this row owns the recognition gate, the two clocks and the wait/stop fork.
Rationale
The wait/stop fork keys on the one ceiling the human consented to, so an unbounded silent sleep is unrepresentable - the fallback nap is capped at the same ceiling for the same reason, and every arm prints what it is waiting for so a throttled run is tellable from a wedged one at the console.

Title
A stalled session ends at its declared limit
SN-Refs

Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall end a session that has made no declared progress within its declared stall limit, reporting the stall as its own outcome rather than as success or as an ordinary failure.
Rationale
MINTED 2026-08-17 (Sol F12, applied), the sibling of SR-171 and from the same defect: the obligation lived only in SR-026's acceptance. Realizes SN-006 and SN-025. It is a SEPARATE row from SR-171 because the two fail independently and for opposite reasons - a backoff that never gives up is the failure this row catches, and a stall limit that fires on a legitimately slow provider is the failure SR-171's bound prevents. Stated as its own outcome because a stall reported as a plain failure sends a reader looking for a defect that is not there, and reported as success is a false green.
AcceptanceCriteria

SR-172
Title
Consecutive no-progress stall guard
Module
project-trajectory/scripts/agent_loop.py
CodeSymbol
LaneState.note_session/main
Detail
Declared progress is a moved git HEAD: note_session resets the stall counter on a commit and increments it otherwise, so the window is consecutive and interleaved progress is never ended by the limit; a throttled (WAITING) session bypasses the counter entirely, and a timed-out one counts (it produced no commit). At --stall-limit consecutive no-commit sessions (a declared flag, default 3) the loop stops with EXIT_STALL (4) - its own exit code, distinct from success and from the error/config exits - under a STALL banner pointing at the latest iteration log. Which banner (the all-ERROR agent-unavailable wording vs the generic work stall) is stall_verdict's fork and LLR-028's home under SR-028; this row owns the counter, the declared limit and the distinct exit.
Rationale
The counter reads committed evidence (HEAD movement), not session prose, so a session cannot talk its way out of the guard - and the reset-on-commit is what keeps the limit a stall detector rather than a slow-progress killer.

The mint commit mirrors the claim's bookkeeping shape; every derived title is deterministic, so the sweep CLI re-run is idempotent.
Rationale

SR SR-175current content
SR-ID
SR-175
Title
Declared inclusion rule for content dispatched to a model provider
SN-Refs
SN-026
Boundary-Refs
B-05
Requirement
The delivered loop content shall compose what it dispatches to an external model runner under a DECLARED inclusion rule rather than an implicit one — the content eligible for each dispatched brief, and what is excluded, readable as a declared set — with the basis on which repository content crosses to a declared provider stated as the scope of the consent the enabling surface grants, never presented as a technical bound on what an invoked runner reads.
Rationale
Hat-derived (hat.DATA-PROTECTION, with hat.SECURITY C-SEC-5 and hat.LEGAL C-LEG-3 converging on the same boundary control — three charters, three reasons, one crossing; clause texts in docs/plans/2026-08-16-blind-derivation-c-hats.md): repository content is briefed to external model providers, and for an adopter that content's history carries every contributor's name, email and timestamp — personal data crossing with no declared basis or exclusion in any need. SN-026's own text governs WHICH model runs, never WHAT IT IS SHOWN, so this obligation is content beyond the parent's demand — the derived-requirement class, labelled so the deriving lens is reviewable and the need owner sees it fed back (ruled at sitting-3 §0.4 item 8, 2026-08-17, adopting the intake proposal, docs/plans/2026-08-17-wi468-obligation-intake-options.md §1 option (b); the SN-026 amendment was the alternative, deliberately not taken alongside — one rule, one home). WHAT EXISTS TODAY IS A DISCIPLINE, NOT A DECLARATION: the push channel is measurably narrow — an allowlist-only planning surface, the project log excluded BY NAME in three composers, zero authorship fields formatted into any prompt — but the rule lives as a convention inside the composing functions, unfalsifiable because no requirement stated it; this row converts it into a stated one and the chain below pins the conventions as they exist. NOT YET MECHANIZED, stated rather than implied: the declared-set surface itself (a per-row inclusion/exclusion field on the provider registry, or a policy dial) and the planted-credential dispatch block in the acceptance are not built. THE PULL-CHANNEL LIMIT, the design-control honesty this row must carry: every enabled runner is launched with permission-bypass flags and can read the whole tree itself, so the declared rule can bound what the loop ASSEMBLES and state the scope of what the consent surface CONSENTS to — it cannot technically bound an invoked runner, the same no-design-control reading the frame applies to external actors at REL-003 and the B-06/B-07 cut. (Derived-requirement label, added 2026-08-17 — PROVISIONAL, unsigned.)
AcceptanceCriteria
The inclusion rule for each brief class the loop dispatches is readable as a declared set naming what is eligible and what is excluded, so a composer can be checked against it rather than against convention; a planted credential inside brief-eligible content blocks dispatch (C-SEC-5's fit criterion, on the same scanner the transcript path already runs); the basis and exclusion for repository content crossing to a declared provider are stated where the provider is declared, and the consent surface's documentation names them as the scope of what enabling consents to; the pull channel is presented as consent-scoped, never as filtered. Read off the current carriers, as the current set: the planning surface's two-registry allowlist read and the disposition/worker composers' by-name exclusions and clipped oneline/name-status log reads — the conventions this row makes declared; the declared-set surface is the row's stated build gap.
